- The real `FieldView3D` was drawn in a window that is not shown, and the figures were looked at from the broadcast and the close-up cameras.

**The user's check and changes (7-1)**
- The user pointed out that on a figure the number overlaps the head, which makes the head's direction hard to see. The cause: the number was 0.53 m (2.40 m) above the top of a cylinder's head (1.87 m), but a figure was 2.22 m (1.2 times). The fix: the number is `LABEL_ABOVE` (0.53 m) above the top of the player's head (the heights in `SceneContent::picks`), and shrinks toward that top when near.
- Decided (user): the figures' scale is an option `figure_scale_3d` (0.8 to 1.5), 1.0 (life size) by default (1.2 in the web version; how to bring it into the web version is for later). Each player's height comes from the kickable area of its type (`SceneBuilder::heightRatio()`; the server's height when it gives one with 2.5D). The cylinders change the height of their bodies by the ratio too. A player whose number the player camera does not know for sure (the opacity of its number under 1, the gray players included) is of the default height (the height would tell which player it is). "Only when gray" was another choice, and the user chose the number.

The cylinders' bodies, heads and direction cones are at the player's height.

Added to `picking` (a taller player's body and head), `overlay_builder` (the line from a taller player's eyes) and `figures_3d` (each figure's height, the scale's range). Breaking what they check makes them fail (17 ways: the number's base, where it shrinks to, the scale option, the figure's height, the scale's range, the rule by the number, the height ratio, the cylinder's body, the head, the two direction cones, the number's height, the figure's height ratio, the figure's picks, the picks' body and head, the line from the eyes). #### 2026-11-14: Step 7-2, walking and running (`feature/3d-view`)

Pushed the 2 commits of the eye height (071894c, e5164d4); CI passed.

**Decided**
- The table of the gait walked (`GaitTable`): for each player, the cycles of the gait walked by each frame (the distance walked over the stride at its speed), found from the log once and kept. A move more than 90° away from the body direction takes the cycles back (the clips play backward). A move farther than 2 m in a cycle, and a player not connected, are not counted. The velocity: each move's velocity put at the middle of the move, joined by lines. The acceleration: the difference of the velocities 2 cycles before and after (0.4 s). When a live game drops its oldest frames, the table drops as many from its front (the other cycles stay). Another log, other strides or another step start it again.
- The strides are those of a figure of a person's height (1.85 m). A taller figure takes longer strides, and so fewer cycles by its height ratio (cycles × 1.85 / height). The mixing weights are by the speed (m/s), whatever the height.
- Reading a clip (`Figures3D::analyzeGait()`): the foot is low in the longest run where the lower of the ankle and the toes is within 15% of its highest lift above its lowest in the clip. In that run, the foot is on the ground in the longest part going back at half the median backward speed or faster. The left foot coming down is phase 0 of the cycle, and the speed at which the feet go back while on the ground (the mean of both feet's medians) is the speed at which the clip does not slide its feet. A clip whose feet do not lift (less than 2% of the model's height) is not read as walking.
- The results (the Mannequin): walk 0.96 m/s (1.28 m a cycle), jog 5.67 m/s (5.29 m), sprint 8.61 m/s (5.74 m). The contacts are all near the start of the clips (0.006, 0.004, 0.033 s). The web version used 1.4, 3.6 and 6.5 m/s, set by eye. The bundled jog is a run with big bounces (the hips go 24 cm up and down), and the speed at which its feet do not slide is 5.7 m/s.
- Mixing: up to 0.8 times the walk's speed, standing and walking are mixed; above it, the two neighboring clips by the speed, linearly. Faster than the sprint, the feet move faster with the sprint's stride. Every clip is read at the same phase (0: the left foot coming down).
- The players' speeds in a real log (the full game log, from the moves of a cycle): 37% below 1 m/s, the rest spread almost evenly over 1 to 9 m/s.
- Going sideways (added to the step's plan): playing the forward walk backward alone looks like sliding sideways. The hips turn toward the way the figure goes (up to 40°), and the chest keeps the body direction (the spine and the chest each turn half back). The turn is largest when the way is 45° from the body direction, and 0 going straight and sideways (where the gait changes between forward and backward): 40° × sin 2δ, so that it does not jump there. Slower than a walk, it is smaller.
- Leaning: toward the acceleration, about the feet, by 10° × tanh(acceleration / 15 m/s²): forward when speeding up, back when slowing down, inward when turning (the centripetal acceleration).
- Turning on the spot (§5.8 item 5) and foot IK (item 6) are not in this step.

**Made**
- `src/view3d/gait_table`: `GaitTable` (`setStrides()`, `strideAt()`, `update()`, `motion()`).
- `FigureMotion` (`scene_content.h`): each figure's cycles walked, velocity and acceleration. `FieldView3D` fills it from the table.
- `Figures3D`: `GaitClip` (the contact time, how long the foot is on the ground, the cycle, the speed, the stride, `timeAt()`), `analyzeGait()`, `gaitClips()`, `strides()`, `mixAt()`, `twistOf()`. `localPose()` adds standing, walking and running, and the hips' turn; `modelMatrix()` adds the lean.
- `HumanoidPose::mix()` (each role the shorter way, the hips' move linearly), `HumanoidRig::turnInModel()` (turns a role's bone in the model, the bones below following).

**Tests**
- Test `gait_table` (new): the stride (between the clips and beyond), the cycles walked (the stride by the speed, between the frames, without a next frame, each player's start, out of range), backward (back beyond 90°), the moves not counted (over 2 m, a player not connected), the velocity and the acceleration (steady, starting, stopping), live (adding bit by bit is the same as building at once, the cycles stay when the front is dropped, found when only the newest is left, built again for other frames, step or strides).
- Added to test `figures_3d`: reading the clips (slowest first, the ranges of the speeds, the strides, in the middle of the time on the ground the left foot lower than the right one and the other way half a cycle later, a clip with its keys turned round by 0.4 s comes down 0.4 s earlier at the same speed, standing is not walking), the mixing weights, the poses between (between standing and walking, between the jog and the sprint, unlike both), the feet do not slide (at the speeds of the 3 clips, for heights 1.85 m and 2.22 m, forward and backward, the left foot moves less than 6 cm × the height ratio in the middle half of its time on the ground; through the gait table too, forward at the jog and backward at the walk), the hips' turn (at 45°, -45°, 135° and 90° the hips turn as much and the chest keeps the body direction, less when slow), leaning (speeding up, slowing down, sideways: the head moves as far as leaned, the feet stay, the limit), standing still keeps the idle clip.
- Added to test `humanoid`: mixing poses (the ends, halfway the shorter way, the hips), turning in the model (turning the hips turns every role; then turning the chest back puts the chest and above back, the hips, legs and spine staying turned).
- Breaking what the tests check makes them fail (21 ways: the backward sign, the 2 m check, the stride by the speed, dropping the front live, the middle time of the velocity, the sign of the acceleration, without a next frame, a change of the step, the cycles by the height, the contact time (the reading and `timeAt()`), the speed condition for being on the ground, the direction of the turn, turning the chest back, the side of the lean, the size of the lean, mixing two clips, mixing in standing, the lift condition, the rotation of the roles above, the shorter way). At first breaking the sign of the x acceleration and the contact time passed (no acceleration along x, and the Mannequin's contacts are all near 0 s); added a stop and a clip with its keys turned round. One change of how the boundary is written behaves the same (equivalent).
- The first version picked the walk clip exactly at the jog's speed (a boundary error); the test of the feet not sliding found it. The sprint counted the part where the foot moves slowly just above the ground before landing as on the ground, and read its speed low (6.3 m/s); fixed with the condition on the backward speed.
- Measurements (Release, work machine): the poses of 22 figures take 0.22 to 0.34 ms a frame (7-1: 0.23 ms; heavier when mixing standing or two clips). The table of a full game log: 4.5 ms for 6,209 frames, 5.7 ms for 8,406 frames.
- Builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (35 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17 tests) and autotools pass too.
- In a window not shown on the screen, drew players of the full game log sprinting (8 to 9 m/s), at a middle speed (3.3 to 4 m/s) and walking (1 m/s), 8 frames each from the close-up camera.

**Remaining**
- Between the walk's and the jog's speeds (1 to 5.7 m/s), two clips whose feet stay on the ground for very different parts of the cycle (the walk about 60%, the jog about 30%) are mixed at the same phase, so the feet slide a little (about 0.4 m a step at 2.5 m/s). Foot IK (§5.8 item 6) can fix it.

**The user's check**
- The user checked it on the screen and decided to commit it for now. The running motion may be adjusted again; the motion in general, not only running, will keep being adjusted (§5.8).

#### 2026-11-14: Step 7-3, actions (`feature/3d-view`)

Pushed the 2 commits of 7-2 (63a07bb, 2861e6c); CI passed.

**Looked into (the full game log)**
- At the frame where a kick bit is set, the ball has already moved about as fast as the kick sent it (rcssserver runs a cycle's commands, then moves the ball). The foot meets the ball at the time of the frame before the bit's first one. The web version put the contact at the bit's frame, one cycle late.
- 747 kicks start, and 596 bits continue a kick of the cycle before (holding the ball, kicking it faster in steps). The ball's speed after a kick: median 0.79 m per cycle, 90% up to 2.27.

**Decided**
- Reading the actions from the log (`FigureActionLog::read()`): from the bits of the frames around the frame drawn (from 2 frames ahead to 16 frames before), the kick, the tackle, being down after a foul (FOUL_CHARGED) and the catch, each as cycles from its moment (the frame before the bit's first frame). A run of frames with a bit is one action. Of the actions around, the latest begun is taken, while it has not ended. Nothing piles up from draw to draw.
- Live, the newest frame is drawn one cycle late, so an action is seen from its moment (not the wind-up), and a new frame can change the pose at the same time (live only).
- The kick: of kicks in a row, the one that sent the ball fastest is the moment. Below 1.0 m per cycle after the kick it is a touch; from 2.0 toward the opponents' goal (within 45 m, passing the post by at most 2 m) a shot; otherwise a pass. The foot is on the side where the ball was before the kick; the swing goes where the ball goes.
- The times (cycles from the moment): a kick from 0.6, 0.9 or 1.0 before (touch, pass, shot) to 0.8, 1.3 or 1.5 after. A tackle from 0.6 before to 2.5 after the bit's last cycle. Down after a foul from 0.6 before to 2.8 after the last cycle. A catch from 1.0 before to 2.0 after catch_ban_cycle. The weights rise and fall within them (eased). A touch keeps some running (weight 0.8).
- The poses: the Mannequin has no clips of actions, so they are made here (from the values of the web version's `pose.ts`): each leg and arm by its swing forward from hanging down, its turn to the right and the bend of the knee or the elbow. The legs in the model's directions (on the ground: a supporting leg stays under the body even if a clip leans the hips), the arms in the chest's.
  - Kick: the kicking leg swings back and up with the knee bent, through the ball at the moment, and on. A shot swings far, a pass in between with the inside of the foot (the leg turned out 30°), a touch little. The other arm goes out (not for a touch). The supporting leg under the body.
  - Tackle: the hips down, sliding with the right leg ahead, the upper body leaning back.
  - Down after a foul: the hips near the ground, the legs ahead, the upper body back, the hands on the ground behind.
  - Catch: by the ball's height (`ball.z`). Low (0.3 m or lower): bending the knees and leaning forward, the hands ahead and down. High (1.5 m or higher): the hands up (as in the web version). The logs of today's server have no height, so it is low (the web version always raised the hands). A ball more than 0.5 m to the side leans the goalie to it about the feet (35° at most, from 2 m; a real dive comes with the clips of Phase 9).
  - Neck: the neck's angle spread 20% on the chest, 40% on the neck, 40% on the head.
  - Pointing: the right arm up to the shoulder's height toward where it points (not while kicking, tackling, down or catching).
  - One body action at a time (a foul, then a tackle, then a kick). For the arms, the catch, then the pointing.

**Made**
- `src/view3d/figure_actions`: `FigureActionLog::read()` and its time constants.
- `scene_content.h`: `KickKind`, `FigureKick`, `FigureRun`, `FigureCatch`, `FigureActions`. `FieldView3D` fills them for each figure.
- `Figures3D`: `weightsOf()`, `curve()`, `rollOf()`, `act()`, `setLeg()`, `setArm()`.
- `HumanoidRig::turnedInModel()`, `setTurnedInModel()` (read and set how a role's bone is turned in the model).

**Tests**
- Test `figure_actions` (new): the kick's moment (the frame before the bit, the times before and after, without a next frame), the foot and the way (by the body direction), kicks in a row at the fastest, of two kicks the latest begun, the kinds (touch, pass, shot, wide of the goal, toward its own goal, too far, the right team), the tackle and the foul (the moment, how long the bit lasted, the time after, a single frame), the catch (where the ball was, its height, catch_ban_cycle), the neck and the arm, a player without bits, out of range.
- Added to test `figures_3d`: the eased curve, the weights (before the lead, at the moment, after the tail, 0.8 for a touch, held while the bit lasts), the neck (the head turns by the neck's angle, the chest a fifth, the hips not at all), the kick (back and up in the backswing, ahead at the moment, ahead and up after it, either foot, the other foot on the ground, the other foot under the body when sprinting too, a shot swinging farther than a pass and a pass than a touch, a pass with the toes turned about 30° to the right, kicked to the left the follow-through to the left), the tackle (the hips low, the right foot ahead, the upper body back), down after a foul (the hips near the ground, the head back, both feet ahead at the ground's height, both hands behind at the ground's height), the catch (for a low ball the hands ahead and low, the feet on the ground and the hips down; for a high ball the hands above the head; toward a ball on the right the hands to the right; leaning to a ball to the side, not while down), pointing (the right hand at the shoulder's height to the right, not while kicking), the poses between in the middle of an action.
- Breaking what the tests check makes them fail (31 ways: the kick's moment, the fastest kick, the kicking foot, the latest begun, the shot's direction, the shot's range, the moment of the tackle and the foul, how long the bit lasted, the catch's side, without a next frame, the neck's chest, the neck's neck, the kicking leg, the pass's foot, the kick's way, the supporting leg, the direction of the turn, the direction of the knee, the direction of the lean after a foul, dropping the hips, the tackle, pointing while kicking, leaning while down, the direction of the lean, the catch's height, the touch's weight, the easing, mixing the foul's pose, the legs in the model's directions, bending for a catch). At first removing the supporting leg passed (in the idle clip the foot is on the ground anyway); added the test of kicking while sprinting. The check that the player is connected behaved the same without it (a player not connected has no bits), so it was removed.
- Errors found on the way: for a low catch the hands pointed back (the arms are set from the chest, and leaning the chest forward turns a hanging arm back; the angle from the chest was made larger). The feet of a player down after a foul were 11 to 29 cm under the ground (the drop of the hips and the angles of the legs were changed). Kicking while running left the supporting foot behind (the sprint clip leans the hips forward; the legs are now set in the model's directions).
- Measurements (Release, work machine): reading the actions takes 0.002 ms a frame for 22 players. The poses of 22 figures with the neck, a kick and a catch take 0.38 ms.
- Builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (36 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17 tests) and autotools pass too.
- In a window not shown on the screen, drew kicks (2 passes), a tackle (its bit lasting 10 frames), a player down after a foul and a goalie's catch of the full game log in slow playback, 8 pictures a cycle.

**Remaining**
- The kicking foot does not always reach the ball (its place is not matched; foot IK can match it). → Matched below.
- A real dive, falling and getting up, celebrating and so on come with the clips of Phase 9 (9-4).

**The user's check and changes (7-3)**
- The user checked it on the screen: the kick's timing and the size of its swing, the poses of the tackle and the foul, the time until getting up, and the neck's turn are fine.
- What the user pointed out and asked for, and what was changed:
  - The fouled player sat down at the same time as the tackle, which looked unnatural. The FOUL_CHARGED bit turns out to come at the same frame as the tackle's bit (all 6 cases in the full game log). Changed: the fouled player falls from one cycle after the action's moment (after the tackler has slid in), in 0.8 cycles (`FOULED_START`, `FOULED_FALL`). Seen live too.
  - For a goalie's catch, the hands should come nearer the ground (the ball's height). Changed: for a low ball the goalie bends at the hips too (the hips' role forward 40°), crouches deep (the thighs forward 60°, the knees 110°, the hips 0.35 of their height down) and puts the arms straight down. The hands are at 0.16 to 0.28 m (before: 0.72 to 0.82 m).
  - Pointing far to a side, the arm went into the body. Changed: the arm on the side it points to points (the right arm to the right, the left arm to the left). Pointing more than 60° to the side or behind, the chest turns toward it by half the rest (60° at most), and the neck turns back so the head keeps its way. The arm is set in the model's directions (the way it points does not change when the neck turns the chest), the elbow a little bent, and lower for a point near the feet (from the shoulder's height, 1.4 m, to the point; no lower than 30°).
  - The kicking foot's toes should come to the ball. Changed: around the kick's moment (from 0.4 cycles before to 0.3 after), the kicking leg is moved by two-bone IK so that the toes go to the back of the ball where it was at the moment (by the radius of the ball as drawn, at its center's height). The weight is 1 at the moment (for a touch too). The knee bends forward and the foot keeps the kick pose's turn; a ball out of reach gets the leg stretched toward it. `FigureKick` now has the ball's place at the moment, the way it goes and the drawn ball's radius.
- Showing the wind-up live too (the user's question: would drawing a few more cycles late solve it?). Answer: the wind-up starts up to a cycle before the action's moment, and the moment is the frame before the bit's, so two cycles must be drawn after the bit's frame arrives. Drawing live two cycles late instead of one shows the wind-ups of the kick, the tackle and the catch as in a log (three cycles to make the lean's acceleration stable too). To be decided with the user.
- Tests: added to and changed in test `figure_actions` (the fouled player's start, the kick's ball place, way and radius, the distance to the point) and `figures_3d` (the fouled player still standing at the moment, halfway down, the hands of a low catch below 0.3 m, pointing (far to the right, near: lower, to the left with the left arm and the right one down, behind with the chest turned, the head not and the hand behind, the same way whatever the neck turns the chest), the foot and the ball (the toes within 3 cm for any kind and either foot, the knee not bending back, stretched toward a far ball, the weight's window, not while tackling)). Breaking what they check makes them fail (18 ways: the foul's start (the reader and the weight), the ball's place, the ball's way, the point's distance, bending at the hips for a catch, the pointing arm's side, keeping the head, turning the chest, lowering the arm, the arm in the model's directions, reaching, reaching for a touch, keeping the foot's turn, not reaching while tackling, the knee bending forward, the ball's back). At first breaking the ball's way along x and the arm in the model's directions passed; added the x of a kick to the side and the way pointed with the neck turned.
- Found on the way: the older kick tests gave no ball place (the foot was pulled to the origin). For a touch, the reach's weight stopped at the pose's weight (0.8). With a nearly straight leg, the side the knee bends to was not settled (it now bends to the figure's front).
- Measurement: the poses take 0.49 ms a frame in the heavy case of all 22 figures kicking and reaching.
- Builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (36 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17 tests) and autotools pass too. Drew the kick, the foul and the catch of the full game log in slow playback.

#### 2026-11-14: Drawing live two cycles late (`feature/3d-view`)

Made the 2 commits of 7-3 (d0b9580, fee89d7) (pushed together with this one).

**Decided (user)**
- Show the wind-ups of the kick, the tackle and the catch live too, as in a log: the 3D view draws a live game two cycles behind its newest frame instead of one (fixed, not a setting). The 2D view draws the newest frame as before.

**Made**
- `Interpolate::LIVE_DELAY` (2) and `Interpolate::position()`: live, from the frame two before the newest toward the next one, by the time since the newest came divided by `simulator_step`. With only 2 or 3 frames yet, as late as they allow (from the first frame toward the next).

**Tests**
- Test `interpolate`: with 10 frames from 7 toward 8, with 2 and 3 frames from 0 toward 1. Putting the delay back to one cycle makes it fail, and so does removing the handling of few frames.
- Builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (36 tests). How it looks live with a real server is for the user to check.
- Keeping the lean from the acceleration unchanged when new frames come would need three cycles (the acceleration is taken from the velocities two cycles before and after). Two cycles late, the lean live can change a little when a new frame comes.

**The user's check, and a defect found**
- The user checked it live: the players move well, but the ball moves jerkily (smoothly in a log).
- Cause: the time drawn live was taken from the time since the newest frame came, but a new frame was found when the view was next drawn (up to 16.7 ms later at 60 fps), and the time was measured from there. At every frame the time drawn jumped by up to ±0.17 cycles (since 4-2 of Phase 4). For the ball, moving 2 to 3 m a cycle, that is a jump of 0.3 to 0.5 m; for the players (1 m or less) it does not show. A log is timed exactly by the player's timer. Drawing two cycles late has nothing to do with it.
- Fixed: a clock for the live view (`Interpolate::LiveClock`). It goes on at the simulation's speed and changes its speed (0 to 2 times, never backward) to catch up, with a time constant of 3 cycles, with the time the frames' coming puts it at (`Interpolate::liveTime()`). It jumps only when 2 cycles or more away (another game, the oldest frames dropped), and goes on at most a cycle from one drawing to the next (after a pause). When the next frame is late, it goes on into the frames there up to the newest (it used to stop a cycle ahead and wait). The frames and the fraction come from `Interpolate::positionAt()`; the figures' poses still come from the frames and the fraction only.
- Tests: added to test `interpolate`: going on up to the newest frame when the next is late, `liveTime()`, `positionAt()`, the clock (drawing 60 times a second, with a frame every 0.1 s found at the next drawing, the time the frames put jumps 0 to 0.33 cycles from one drawing to the next, while the clock goes on by 0.12 to 0.22 and stays around two before the newest; ahead, it slows down and does not go back; far, it jumps; not beyond the newest; at most a cycle at once). Removing the clock, the catching up, the limit at once, the limit at the newest, or going on into the frames there each makes it fail.
- Builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (36 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17 tests) passes too.

#### 2026-11-14: Step 7-4, finishing (`feature/3d-view`)

The two commits of the live view (d1f53d8, 02c896f) were pushed with the two of 7-3, and CI passed.

**Decided**
- Hit testing: the radius of a figure's body cylinder is 1.2 times the player's size (player_size; as the web version) times the figure's height over a person's (§5.8). A figure of a larger scale and a taller player are wider. The cylinders' players are as before.
- The figures' models: the values of `player_model_3d` are simple / mannequin / human (7-1's figure is read as mannequin). The human body (`human`) is the second bundled figure (D14). The default stays simple (§11, 13).
- The human body's material: the male body (`Superhero_Male_FullBody`), the eyebrows and the eyes of the Universal Base Characters [Standard] (CC0), with the hairstyle `Hair_SimpleParted` of the same pack, kept with the attributes used (positions, normals, joints, weights, triangles) in one .glb (`resources/models/BaseCharacter.glb`, 524 kB, about 15,600 triangles). The script that wrote it, `pack_base_character.py` (Python's standard library only), is beside it. No reading of .gltf and .bin was added; the bundle is one file (reading .gltf directly comes with the figure packs in Phase 9). The hair's armature has other inverse bind matrices than the body's in the arms and the legs, but the same for the root and the Head, which the hair uses, so it joins the body's skin. No textures: the eyebrows and the hair are dark brown, the eyes dark, each one color. The body has 65 bones, of the same names and order as the mannequin's. Both are in a T pose; the differences of the bones' directions at rest (up to 17° at the neck, 14° in the spine) are taken by the humanoid layer.
- The motion and the body drawn apart: the motion is the mannequin's clips (walking and running analyzed on the mannequin), the body drawn the mannequin or the human body (`Figures3D::setModel()`, read when first used). The clips' poses go on the body through the humanoid layer, and the neck, the poses of the actions and the foot IK are made on the body drawn. The bones without a role (the fingers) are turned from the body's rest as the mannequin's are from theirs.
- The uniform (`FigureUniform`): no texture painted, so that any humanoid body can wear it. Each vertex is on a part of the body by the bones' weights (the torso, the left and right arms and legs, the head; a bone without a role counts for the role above it), and the edges of the clothes are planes from the bones at rest: the sleeves at 0.4 of the upper arms, the shorts' hems at 0.5 of the thighs, the socks 0.12 below the knees, the shoes 0.08 from the ankles up to the knees, the collar at the root of the neck, the waist halfway between the hips and the spine. The colors: the shirt and the socks in the team color (as a cylinder's body, with the state and stamina colors), the shorts 0.4 times it, the shoes nearly black, the skin one color. The fragment shader (`skinned.frag`) decides the same for every pixel with the point at rest carried from the vertices, so the edges are straight, not along the triangles. The mannequin is as before (all in the team color, the joints in the model's). The skin is not of each player's own color: the player camera would know a player whose number it is not sure of (as by the height).
- The body's strides: of the same height, the human body has longer legs than the mannequin, and its feet slid with the mannequin's strides. The ratio of the legs (the hips' height over the height, 1.035) was tried first, but did not lessen the slip going forward. The feet with the clips put on the body are now measured directly by the analysis of walking and running (`analyzeGait()` takes a body), and the mean of the ratios of the speeds to the mannequin's (walk 1.044, jog 1.035, sprint 1.038), 1.039, scales the body's strides (`Figures3D::bodyStride()`). The mix is by the speed as before.
- Building a frame moved from FieldView3D to `FrameBuilder3D`, without GL (the show between the frames, the goals and the boards, the players with the figures' gaits, actions and poses, the overlays, what the cameras follow, the player camera's perception). FieldView3D keeps the time (where between the frames), the camera, the labels, the drawing and the HUD. The inspection state `View3DState` (§10.2: the frames and the fraction drawn, the camera, the players, cylinders and figures drawn, the labels, the figures' model, the actions, each figure's left knee and head turn, the skin matrices) comes from `FrameBuilder3D::state()` and `FieldView3D::state()`. A test without a screen cannot make the OpenGL widget, so the tests use FrameBuilder3D.
- The measuring (§10.3): started with the environment variable `RCSSMONITOR_3D_TIMING`, the 3D view tells its times on the standard error every 2 seconds. The GPU's time is by OpenGL's timer queries (`gl/gpu_timer`), read some frames later without waiting for the GPU.
- The settings dialog: Players between Guides and Stadium in the 3D tab (the three Models with their summary, and Figure Scale). Figure Scale is dimmed with Simple. The Default button sets these two back too.

**Made**
- `SceneBuilder::FIGURE_PICK_SCALE` and the radius of a figure's hit testing.
- `PlayerModel::Mannequin`, `Human` (`camera_mode.h`).
- `GltfModel`: each vertex's point at rest (`SkinVertex::rest`) and part (`part`).
- `src/view3d/figure_uniform`: `paintParts()`, `cutsOf()`, `regionAt()`, `colorOf()`, `Cuts`.
- `Figures3D`: `setModel()`, `bodyModel()`, `bodyStride()`, `jerseyMaterials()`, `uniformMaterials()`, `uniformCuts()`, `motionModel()`, `motionRig()`, `worldNodes()`, reading a body (`Body`). The arguments of a body to `analyzeGait()`.
- `SkinnedMesh`: how each material is painted (`uPaint`: its color, the player's, the uniform), the vertex attributes `A_REST`, `A_PART`. The shader `skinned.frag` (lit as `lit.frag`). The uniform's materials and edges to `Scene3D::setFigureModel()`.
- `src/view3d/frame_builder_3d`: `FrameBuilder3D`, `View3DState`, `FigureProbe`.
- `GpuTimer`, `Scene3D::setTiming()`, `takeGpuTime()`, the timing output of `FieldView3D`.
- Players of `ConfigDialog`; the figures' model and scale in `Options::setView3DDefaults()`.

**Tests**
- Test `frame_builder_3d` (new): on a made log of 22 players running at their own speeds, with a kick and a tackle: the players, figures and cylinders drawn (the top camera, simple, no players shown, no frames), the position between the frames, the left knees, the actions counted, the same pose for a frame (drawn again and again, stepped forward and back, jumped far and back, drawn straight by another builder, live with frames coming later: the skin matrices, the left knees and the heads' turns equal), what the cameras follow and the player camera's perception. It checks "the same pose" of Phase 7's completion conditions.
- Test `figure_uniform` (new): the planes, what a point wears by its part and the planes (sleeves, shorts, knees, socks, shoes, collar, waist, head), the colors, 12 points of the human body (upper arm, forearm, finger, chest, back, belly, thigh, knee, shin, toes, face, top of the head) with their parts and clothes, the order of the planes down a leg, along an arm and up the torso.
- Added to test `figures_3d`: the human body (reading, the joints, the height, the materials, the stride's scale being the mean of the speed ratios measured on the body, the feet on the ground, the head's height, the same motion as the mannequin's, the left foot sliding less than 2.5 cm walking and running (forward and backward), the kicking foot within 3 cm of the ball, the hands and feet near the ground sitting after a foul, the skin matrices, back to the mannequin), the models' names.
- Added to test `scene_3d`: the human body drawn from its side, compared with the same pixels drawn without the uniform (all in the player's color): the shirt and the sock the player's color as bright, the shorts 0.4 times it, the shoe dark, the face and the forearm the skin's color. The light differs with the faces' directions, so pixels of the same face are compared (at first fixed brightnesses were compared, which failed on a face in the shade).
- Added to tests `scene_builder` (the radius of a figure's hit testing) and `config_dialog` (the items of Players, dimmed with Simple, Default).
- Every test was checked to fail when what it checks is broken for a moment (8 ways in FrameBuilder3D, 15 in the human body and the uniform, 4 in the dialog). At first, removing the interpolation in FrameBuilder3D and not updating the table of the gaits went unnoticed, so the position between the frames and the pose of frames coming later live were added. Removing the human body's strides still passed within the feet's tolerance (the mannequin's 6 cm), so the human body's is 2.5 cm (1.5 cm at most with its strides, 3.5 to 3.9 cm without). Breaking how a vertex's point at rest is found changes nothing on the human body, whose vertices are at rest already; test `gltf_model` catches it on the mannequin (quantized vertices).
- Measured (Release, the work machine, its integrated GPU (radeonsi), `RCSSMONITOR_3D_TIMING`): 1920×1080 logical (2480×1395 physical pixels at the screen's scale 1.29), the broadcast camera, the whole-game log played at normal speed, drawn into a window not shown every 16 ms (the fps stops at 62.5 by that interval).

| Figures | CPU to build | of it, the players and figures | GPU to draw |
|---|---|---|---|
| simple (cylinders) | 0.06 to 0.07 ms | — | 0.8 to 1.0 ms |
| mannequin (22) | 0.70 to 0.72 ms | 0.66 to 0.67 ms | 1.5 to 1.7 ms |
| human (22) | 0.76 to 0.78 ms | 0.72 to 0.74 ms | 2.0 to 2.2 ms |

  Reading the figures takes 5 to 7 ms for the motion and the mannequin, and 9 to 15 ms when the human body is first used (with measuring its strides).
- Builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (38 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17 tests) and autotools pass too.
- The human bodies of the whole-game log were drawn in a window not shown, from the close-up camera (3.5 m and 6 m) and from afar (25 m), and looked at.

**Left**
