#### 2026-11-14: Step 7-5, the human bodies' individuality (`feature/3d-view`)

The two commits of 7-4 (5f0b552, f522bf3) were pushed, and CI passed.

**Decided (user)**
- The human bodies' skin and hair colors differ by the player. The body too is chosen for each player from the models available. The woman's body is mixed in, and mixed, men only or women only is an option.
- The player camera knowing a player by its looks is handled by bringing the looks toward the standard ones by how sure it is of the number (the user's idea was blending with the standard colors from afar; the blend is by the opacity of the number, which falls with the distance by the server's rules, rather than by the distance itself). The height is mixed by the same, not switched.

**Decided**
- The material: the free version (Standard) has one man's and one woman's body. The hairstyles are made for each head (checked by the inverse bind matrix of the head bone): SimpleParted, Buzzed and Beard for the man's, Buns, Long and BuzzedFemale for the woman's. Each body has its own hairstyles with it, in two files, the man's (`BaseCharacterMale.glb`, 585 kB, about 17,500 triangles) and the woman's (`BaseCharacterFemale.glb`, 858 kB, about 22,100 triangles), replacing 7-4's `BaseCharacter.glb`. The materials are named for how they are drawn, `Body`, `Eyes`, `Eyebrows`, `Hair_<style>` (by the script that writes them).
- The looks (`FigureLooks`): the body (half and half when mixed), the hairstyle (a man SimpleParted or Buzzed half and half, a beard for 3 in 10; a woman Buns, Long or BuzzedFemale, a third each), the skin color (between two neighbors of six colors from light to dark), the hair color (black 3 in 10, dark brown 3 in 10, brown, dark blond, blond, red). They are drawn by numbers seeded with a hash of the team's name, the side and the number: random numbers anew would change the looks of the same frame, against the principle of §5.2. The same player of the same team looks the same in every game.
- The player camera: by the opacity c of the number, the skin and hair colors are between the standard ones (7-4's) and the player's own (c of the way), and with c under 1 the body and the hairstyle are the standard ones (a man's SimpleParted; BuzzedFemale with women only). The height ratio is 1 + (the type's ratio − 1) × c too (figures and cylinders). With the other cameras, always the own looks and height.
- The drawing: each player's skin and hair colors and the hairstyles it wears (bits) are attributes of its instance (`A_SKIN`, `A_HAIR`; the hairstyles in the w of the hair color). On a player not wearing a hairstyle, the vertex shader puts the hairstyle's triangles out of sight (beyond the far plane). Within OpenGL 3.3, as many draw calls as hairstyles (a few). Each body has its skinned mesh, and draws its players in one instanced draw call (`SceneContent::figure_bodies`).
- Each body's strides: the woman's body too has the mean of the speed ratios measured with the clips on it (1.041) as its scale (the man's 1.039).
- The option `figure_bodies_3d` (mixed / male / female, mixed by default) and `--figure-bodies-3d` on the command line. Bodies (three choices) in Players of the settings dialog, chosen with Human only.

**Made**
- `src/view3d/figure_looks`: `FigureLook`, `FigureLooks::of()`, `standard()`, `blend()`, `hairBit()`, the hairstyles' bits.
- `FigureBodies` (`camera_mode.h`), `figure_bodies_3d` of `Options`, Bodies in the dialog.
- `SceneBuilder::addShow()`: the figures' looks, and the height mixed by how sure the number is.
- `Figures3D`: three bodies (the mannequin, the man's, the woman's), `bodyCount()`, `bodyOf()`, `paints()`, each body's `model()`, `rig()`, `bodyStride()`, `uniformCuts()`. The poses, the actions and the foot IK are made on the player's body.
- `FigureUniform::Paint` (how a material is painted: its color, the player's, the uniform, the hair's with a hairstyle's bit). The skin's color to `colorOf()`.
- `SkinnedMesh::Instance` (the player's color, skin, hair), `Scene3D::setFigureBody()` (by the body). The skin, the hair and the hairstyles in `skinned.vert` and `skinned.frag`.
- `View3DState::figure_bodies`.

**Tests**
- Test `figure_looks` (new): the hairstyles' names and bits, the standard looks, the same looks for the same player and others for the others, the body by the option with its own hairstyles, of 198 players women 35 to 65% and beards for 15 to 45% of the men, all 5 hairstyles and 6 hair colors drawn, the range of the skins (light and dark ones), the blend by the certainty (the own at 1, the standard at 0, only the colors between).
- Added to test `scene_builder`: the figures' looks the players' own (three options), men and women mixed, with the player camera half sure of the number the colors half way and the standard body and hair, the height half way (cylinder, hit testing, figure), the default height not sure at all.
- Added to test `figures_3d`: the woman's body (height, materials, the strides' scale the mean of the measured ratios and other than the man's, the feet on the ground, the left foot sliding less than 2.5 cm walking and running), choosing the body (a number out of range kept within), how the materials are painted with the hairstyles' bits, the skin matrices and the bodies of a man and a woman, one body back to the mannequin. The options' names.
- Added to test `scene_3d`: the skin and the hair in the player's colors, only the eyebrows without a hairstyle, more with a beard, nothing of a woman's hairstyle asked for on the man's body, the woman's body drawn with long hair.
- Added to test `frame_builder_3d`: both bodies drawn for the human bodies mixed, one body for men or women only, the knees, the same pose jumped far and back.
- Added to test `config_dialog`: the items of Bodies, chosen with Human only, Default.
- Every test was checked to fail when what it checks is broken for a moment (14 ways: the team's name in the seed, the beard, the body not for sure, the height's mix, the looks' blend, choosing the body, the hairstyles shown or not (the materials and the shader), the skin's color, drawing by the body, the dialog, the hair's color, the woman's strides). Using the man's strides' scale for the woman's body passed: the two differ by 0.2%, which the feet's slide does not show (nearly the same). Instead, the woman's scale is checked to be the mean of the ratios measured on her body.
- Measured (as in 7-4): 22 human bodies (mixed), the CPU builds in 0.80 ms (the players and figures 0.75 ms), the GPU draws in 2.3 to 2.4 ms (2.0 to 2.2 ms with men only in 7-4). Reading the human bodies when first used takes about 18 ms for the two.
- Builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (39 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17 tests) and autotools pass too.
- Four players of the whole-game log were drawn in a window not shown from the close-up camera and looked at (the skins, a beard, the woman's body and hairstyle).

**The user's check, and a bug found (7-5)**
- The user checked it on the screen: the random looks are fine.
- The user pointed out: on the man's body, the uniform looked torn from the neck to the shoulders. Two causes. (1) The part of the body (the head, the torso, an arm, a leg) was decided by the vertex, and a triangle took its first vertex's part (not interpolated): at the neck and the shoulders, a triangle with a vertex moved mostly by the head was all skin, jagged along the triangles. (2) The collar was one plane at the neck's root, so the shoulders rising above it (the man's trapezius is 7 cm above the neck's root even 13 cm from the neck) were skin.
- Fixed: no part of the head; the head is on the torso. The arms and the legs by the bones' weights interpolated between the vertices, decided for every pixel at a half (the side by the plane between the shoulders). The collar: the skin within a cone round the neck's middle line (wider higher up), above the neck's root (down to 4 cm below it), and all above 0.4 of the neck's length (the chin, the head). The middle line along the bones through the middle of the skin across the neck at 0.7 of its length; the collar's radius 1.3 times the median distance of those points from it, widening by half the height. At first the farthest distance across the neck's middle was taken, which on the man's body included the trapezius (0.115 m) and made the shoulders' tops skin; widening by the height at first, the man's high trapezius was skin too.
- Tests: test `figure_uniform` was fixed (deciding the parts, points of both bodies, the front and the back of the neck skin, the tops of both shoulders (the highest vertex of the skin at 0.6, 0.7 and 0.8 of the way from the neck to the shoulder) in the shirt, the collar's width, the cone's widening, above the chin). Added to test `scene_3d`: drawn from the front above, both shoulders' tops in the shirt, under the chin the skin. Breaking what they check makes them fail (11 ways: the collar a plane only (C++ and the shader), the farthest distance, measuring at the neck's middle, the sides swapped, above the chin (C++), the widening, the head counted as an arm, the shader's sides). Removing the shader's test above the chin changes nothing on these two bodies, whose chins and faces are within the collar's cone (the same; it is for bodies with larger heads). Builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (39 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17 tests) and autotools pass too.
- Found: the woman's hairstyle BuzzedFemale has holes at the back of the head where the scalp shows (gaps in the material's mesh; the original paints the scalp by its texture). All the materials are double-sided, but the holes stayed with the figures drawn two-sided: not the culled back faces.
- Decided (user): the scalp where the hair is is painted in the hair's color.
- Fixed (`FigureUniform::paintScalp()`): when read, for each hairstyle, the vertices of the skin within 1.2 cm of its triangles with the hair outward of the skin (within 60 degrees of its normal) are marked (within 3 mm, whichever way). Without the outward condition, the skin beyond the hair's edge was marked too, and a band of the hair's color stood out about 1 cm below the short hair at the nape. The marks are a vertex attribute (`A_SCALP`, up to 4 hairstyles), interpolated for every pixel; where the mark of a hairstyle the player wears is over a half, the hair's color is painted (a smooth hairline). The chin under the beard too. The long hair is away from the scalp and marks none, but covers it itself. The marking looks only at the hair's triangles near a vertex (in a grid): reading the human bodies when first used takes about 30 ms (about 120 ms trying all).
- Tests: added to test `figure_uniform` (on a made skin and hair: under the hair, beyond its edge, far below, near but facing away; on both bodies: the back of the head under the short hairstyles, the face and the nape not under any, the chin under the beard, the hairstyles' bits). Added to test `scene_3d`: the woman drawn from behind shows no scalp at the back of her head, and does without a hairstyle. Breaking what they check makes them fail (6 ways: the outward condition, the near condition, the shader painting never or always, the bits, the vertex attribute). At first, removing the outward condition passed (the made point "beyond the edge" was farther than 1.2 cm), so it was put 1.1 cm from the hair's corner. Measured: 22 human bodies (mixed), the GPU 2.7 to 3.0 ms, the CPU 0.80 to 0.84 ms. Builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (39 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17 tests) and autotools pass too. The six hairstyles were drawn in light blond from the front, the back and the sides, and looked at.

#### 2026-11-15: The end of Phase 7, and the parallel builds of the CI (`feature/3d-view`)

The 2 commits of 7-5 (1f45db8, 7930436) were pushed.

**A bug of the CI**
- The job CMake, Qt6 (Ubuntu 26.04) stopped in the middle of the build. The first time, it was stopped from outside after about 40 seconds (exit code 143); the second, rerun, made no progress for over 17 minutes and was cancelled. The other 3 jobs passed. No compile error was shown.
- The cause: the CI built with `cmake --build build --parallel`, without a number. Then the Makefile build gets `make -j` with no limit, and the compiling of more than 30 files starts within a second (seen in the log). With the files added in 7-5, the runner ran out of memory in the job of Qt 6.10 and GCC 15, whose headers are heavy (written the same way since 4-1, it had been enough until now).
- Fixed: the 2 CMake jobs build with `cmake --build build --parallel "$(nproc)"` (the autotools job has used `make -j"$(nproc)"` from before). With commit 7e2c99e, the 4 jobs passed (CMake, Qt6 in about 3 minutes).

**Phase 7's completion conditions**
- The user checked on real logs the figures walking, running, kicking, tackling (for a moment on a foul), sitting down when fouled, catching and pointing (7-2, 7-3).
- Test `frame_builder_3d` checks the same pose (the skin matrices, the left knee, the head's turn) stepped forward and back, jumped and back, and drawn again and again (7-4, 7-5).
- The user checked 7-5 on the screen. Phase 7's completion conditions are met.

### Phase 8

#### 2026-11-15: Step 8-1, shadows (`feature/3d-view`)

The 1 commit of the plan (13223e6) was pushed.

**Decided**
- Phase 8 in 5 steps: 8-1 shadows, 8-2 grass stripes, sky and fog and the ground outside, 8-3 stands, 8-4 the ball's pattern and rolling, 8-5 the ball ring and trail, and a measurement with everything on. Each step adds its settings and its items of the dialog.
- The shadows as in the web version (§4.3): from the sun's direction (-60, 65, -30), the players' bodies and heads, the figures, and the goal posts and crossbars cast shadows on the ground. Only the ground receives them (none on the players or the goals). A plane that draws only the shadows (over the ground image and 4 m around it) is 0.01 m over the ground, and darkens the shadows with black at 0.4. The ball keeps its disc right below (it casts no shadow of the sun), and the direction cones cast none either. Not drawn from the top camera, nor with the option off.
- The shadow map is 4096 px (where the GPU takes textures of 8192 px; 2048 px otherwise). The sun's camera is orthographic, just over the area of the shadows up to 4 m from the ground (3 m in the web version; the figure of the tallest type at the largest scale, both hands raised to catch, is about 3.6 m).
- The shadows' edges are softened by 25 reads (5 × 5) of the map; but 5 reads come first, the middle and the 4 corners, and the rest are skipped where they are all lit or all in the shadow (the measurements below).
- The ball is apart from the heads in the lists of what is drawn (`SceneContent::balls`), not to be drawn into the shadow map.
- The option `shadows_3d` (on by default; in the group `View3D`) and `--shadows-3d` on the command line. Shadows in Players of the 3D tab of the settings dialog (always available: the cylinders cast them too). Default turns them on. → Made a choice of three below (off / simple / detailed).

**Made**
- `src/view3d/sun_3d`: `Sun3D::direction()`, `shadowLength()`, `shadowWidth()`, `shadowViewProj()` (the 8 corners of the box of the shadows seen from the sun, the orthographic projection fitted to them), the constants (`SHADOW_MARGIN`, `SHADOW_HEIGHT`, `SHADOW_OPACITY`, `SHADOW_PLANE_Y`). No GL.
- `src/view3d/gl/shadow_map`: a depth texture (24 bits, read by comparing as a `sampler2DShadow`, lit outside it) and a framebuffer without colors. `begin()`, `end()`, `bind()`.
- The shaders `depth.vert`, `depth.frag` (depths only; the figures with `skinned.vert` and `depth.frag`: hairstyles not worn cast no shadow either), `shadow.vert`, `shadow.frag` (the plane of the shadows).
- `Scene3D`: at the start of a frame, a pass that draws what casts shadows into the shadow map from the sun. The plane of the shadows first in the translucent pass (under the circles at the feet). Sorting the figures by their bodies is `sortFigures()` and drawing them `drawFigures()`, used for both the shadow map and the scene. The shadow map is on texture unit 1, and `GLFrame::end()` unbinds unit 1 too.
- `SceneContent::shadows` (FrameBuilder3D decides it by the option and the camera not being the top one), `View3DState::shadows`.
- `shadows_3d` of `Options`, Shadows of `ConfigDialog`.

**Tests**
- Test `sun_3d` (new): the sun's direction (of length 1, 44 degrees up, on the far left), the area of the shadows, the sun's camera (the box of the shadows within OpenGL's cube, touching its sides across and down; a point and where the sun's ray through it meets the ground at the same place of the map, the point nearer to the sun; the shadow falls away from the sun, to the right and the near side), the height that casts shadows above 1.2 times the tallest figure.
- Added to test `scene_3d`: seen from straight above, the shadows of the middle of a player's body and of a figure's chest darken the ground away from the sun (0.4 to 0.8 times the lightness without shadows), the pixels toward the sun the same as without, the ground where the ray passes above the figure's head lit, no shadow of the ball in the air, the shadow of a goal post (from 10 m above), no shadow left when the figure is gone (the map cleared every time), the image as before with the shadows off again, the first frame with the shadows the same as the next.
- Added to test `frame_builder_3d`: with the option on, the shadows from the broadcast, player and free cameras, not from the top camera; none with the option off; reset by `clear()`.
- Added to test `config_dialog`: the tooltip of Shadows, on at first, the option changed by a click, back by Default. Test `scene_builder` was fixed for the list of the ball.
- Every test was checked to fail with what it checks broken for a while (11 ways: the plane of the shadows not drawn, the ball casting too, the figures casting none, the cylinders casting none, the map not cleared, drawn from the top camera too, not reset by `clear()`, not back by Default, the sun's camera looking from the other way, the shadows black, the dialog not changing the option). The softness of the edges (the 25 reads) is not checked.
- Measured (Release, the work machine's integrated GPU (radeonsi), `RCSSMONITOR_3D_TIMING`, as in 7-4: logical 1920×1080 (physical 2480×1395), the broadcast camera, playing the full game's log from frame 3,000): cylinders, GPU 0.9 to 1.0 ms → 2.2 to 2.5 ms with the shadows; 22 human bodies, 2.6 to 2.8 ms → 4.8 to 4.9 ms. At first, always reading 25 times, 7.7 to 9.1 ms and 10 to 11.6 ms (about 7 ms for the plane of the shadows). Drawing the shadow map takes about 0.1 to 0.2 ms. Before and after the 5 reads first, the images differ only in a few pixels of the shadows' edges (10 levels at most). The CPU's time does not change.
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the clean builds and the tests pass (40 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17) and autotools also pass.
- In a window not shown on the screen, frame 3,000 of the full game's log was drawn from the broadcast, close-up and goal cameras, with and without the shadows, and looked at.

**The user's proposal and the change (8-1)**
- The user's proposal: the players' shadows switched by the option between detailed ones of the models' shapes and simple circles or ellipses. Decided (user): the option `shadows_3d` is a choice of three, `off` / `simple` / `detailed`, `detailed` by default (the web version's look). → `simple` by default (user: light on any GPU). The web version gets the same three (§0.2). The simple shadows are ellipses stretched away from the sun. With the simple shadows, the goal frames cast none (thin bars drawn as ellipses would look odd).
- The simple shadows (`simple`): an ellipse for each player drawn, as high as the plane of the shadows (0.01 m) (`Sun3D::blobShadow()`). From the feet away from the sun, as long as to where the sun's ray over the top of the head meets the ground (the height × cot 44°: about the player's height), as wide as the body (the cylinder's diameter, or the body's diameter × the height over a person's for a figure), with half the width more at each end. Black at 0.4 out to 0.35 of the way from the middle, fading toward the edge from there (the shader `blob`). As tall as the player is drawn (its type, the scale, the player camera's blend). Not from the top camera. Drawn with the disc of the circles at the feet, in one instanced draw call.
- Made: `ShadowMode`, `shadowModes()`, `shadowModeName()`, `shadowModeFromName()` (`camera_mode.h`). `Sun3D::blobShadow()`, `SceneBuilder::addBlobShadows()`, `SceneContent::blob_shadows`, `PlayerPick::figure` (whether a figure: its pick is 1.2 times as wide as its body, which the shadow's width needs), `View3DState::blob_shadows`, the shaders `blob.vert`, `blob.frag`. Shadows of the settings dialog became a row of three radio buttons with a summary (under Model and Bodies). `--shadows-3d` on the command line takes `off`, `simple` or `detailed`.
- Tests: `sun_3d` (the ellipse's ends half the width behind the feet and half the width beyond the top's shadow, the middle, the width, on the ground, about the height long to the right and the near side), `scene_builder` (one for each player drawn, the widths and heights of the cylinders and the figures, the color, none without players drawn), `scene_3d` (seen from straight above, the middle of the ellipse 0.5 to 0.7 times the lightness without it, lighter near the edge, the pixels outside and toward the sun the same as without, the same in every frame), `frame_builder_3d` (detailed: the shadow map only; simple: the ellipses only, as many as the players (none of the goal frames); the top camera, no players drawn, off, `clear()`), `config_dialog` (the three buttons and their tooltips, Simple at first, Detailed and Off chosen, Simple back by Default). Checked to fail with what they check broken (10 ways: the ellipses not drawn, stretched toward the sun, not fading at the edge, the figures' width from the pick's radius, drawn from the top camera too, the ellipses with detailed too, the dialog's choices mismatched, not the top of the head, not reset by `clear()`, the dialog not showing the option). Toward the sun is caught by `sun_3d` (`scene_3d` takes its points from the ellipse's matrix, so it passes either way).
- Measured (as before): cylinders, GPU 0.84 to 0.94 ms (off), 0.88 to 0.98 ms (simple), 2.1 to 2.3 ms (detailed); 22 human bodies, 2.6 ms, 2.7 ms, 4.9 to 5.0 ms. The simple shadows take 0.04 to 0.1 ms. The CPU's time does not change.
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the clean builds and the tests pass (40 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17) and autotools also pass. In a window not shown on the screen, frame 3,000 of the full game's log was drawn from the broadcast and close-up cameras with each of the three options, and looked at.

#### 2026-11-15: Step 8-2, grass stripes, sky and haze, the ground beyond (`feature/3d-view`)

The 2 commits of 8-1 (72348d6, 3505257) were pushed, and the CI passed.

**Decided**
- As in the web version (`scenery.ts`, §4.3).
- Grass stripes: 5.25 m wide (20 on the pitch), from the halfway line light, dark and so on to the right (the stripes differ left and right of it, as the mower turns there). The light stripes are the field color times 1.05, the dark ones times 0.92. The 2D `FieldPainter` draws them into the ground image, after the grass and before the lines (over the margins too). Not on the minimap.
- Sky: a sphere of 1900 m around the camera. Up to the zenith's #3d7ccc, #d4e4f0 at the horizon (changing faster near the horizon: the height to the power 0.55), below the horizon toward #6f9f7f (all of it 1/12 down).
- The ground beyond: a flat ground out to 1500 m from the center (3000 m square) beyond the ground image, 2 cm under it, in the field color times 0.86 (the web version's #1a8a1a for the default field color #1fa01f). With the sky, it melts into the horizon's color from 600 to 1500 m from the camera (the haze, linearly). The ground beyond is drawn whatever the sky option; without the sky, the background is #87a96b and there is no haze (as in the web version). The haze is only over the ground beyond (the stadium's things are nearer than 600 m).
- The order: the ground beyond is drawn as a solid before the ground image (a depth buffer of 24 bits tells 5 mm apart at 200 m: 2 cm is plenty). The sky is drawn after the solids (the players, the goals, the boards and the banners), testing the depth without writing it (only where nothing is). At first, as in the web version, the sky came first without the depth; it painted the whole screen though the ground covers most of it, about 0.3 ms more of an integrated GPU (the measurements below).
- The options `grass_stripes_3d` and `sky_3d` (both on by default; in the group `View3D`) and `--grass-stripes-3d`, `--sky-3d` on the command line. Grass Stripes and Sky in Stadium of the 3D tab of the settings dialog. Default turns them on.

**Made**
- The parameter `stripes` of `FieldPainter::drawGround()`, `drawGrassStripes()`, the constants `GRASS_STRIPE_WIDTH`, `GRASS_LIGHT`, `GRASS_DARK`. `GroundTexture::draw()` draws the stripes by the option (not at the minimap's size), and `key()` has the option.
- `src/view3d/scenery_3d`: the constants of the sky, the haze and the ground beyond, `skyColor()` (as sky.frag), `haze()` (as outside.frag), `farGroundColor()`. No GL.
- The shaders `sky.vert`, `sky.frag`, `outside.vert`, `outside.frag`. `RenderState::sky()`. `Scene3D`: a pass of the ground beyond (before the ground) and of the sky (after the solids). The background is cleared to the horizon's color with the sky, #87a96b without.
- `SceneContent::sky`, `far_ground`, `View3DState::sky`. The two options of `Options` and `ConfigDialog`.

**Tests**
- Added to test `ground_texture`: the stripes (the middles of 20 stripes light and dark in turn, in the margins too, the edges of a stripe, the lines over them, the field color with the option off, no stripes on the minimap), the key changing with the option. The checks from before are done without the stripes.
- Test `scenery_3d` (new): the sky's colors (the zenith straight up, the horizon level, #6f9f7f below, bluer all the way up from the horizon and faster near it, halfway halfway down), the haze (0 out to 600 m, 1 from 1500 m, linear between, not reaching the stadium), the color of the ground beyond (#1a8a1a for the default field color).
- Added to test `scene_3d`: seen level from 50 m up, the sky's colors at three heights over the horizon as `skyColor()`, the ground beyond 300 m on in its color, 1000 m on toward the horizon's color by the haze, without the sky the background and no haze, the same in every frame. The pixels checked before compare the ground with the field color, so they are drawn without the stripes (the stripes are checked by `ground_texture`).
- Added to tests `frame_builder_3d` (the sky option from every camera, the color of the ground beyond, `clear()`) and `config_dialog` (the tooltips of the two checkboxes, on at first, the options changed by a click, back by Default).
- Every test was checked to fail with what it checks broken for a while (13 ways: no stripes, the stripes' light and dark swapped, the stripes over the lines, the option not in the key, stripes on the minimap, no sky, the sky's gradient linear, no haze with the sky, haze without the sky, no ground beyond, FrameBuilder3D ignoring the sky option, the dialog not changing the stripes, Default not turning the sky back on).
- Measured (as in 8-1, 22 human bodies, the simple shadows): GPU 3.0 to 3.1 ms (no sky and stripes; the ground beyond drawn) → 3.3 ms (with them); 3.6 ms with the sky drawn first. Before and after the change of the order, the images differ only in 131 pixels of the goal camera (7 levels at most; the far corners of the ground beyond). The 0.3 ms over 8-1's 2.7 ms are the ground beyond.
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the clean builds and the tests pass (41 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17) and autotools also pass.
- In a window not shown on the screen, frame 3,000 of the full game's log was drawn from the broadcast, goal and player cameras, and looked at. From the broadcast camera, the ground beyond shows wide beyond the boards (as in the web version; the stands (8-3) fill it).

#### 2026-11-15: Step 8-2b, realistic grass (`feature/3d-view`)

The 2 commits of 8-2 (8e1f8cf, d218678) were pushed, and the CI passed.

**Decided (user)**
- A more realistic grass, chosen by the option. 8-2 committed first, this a step of its own (8-2b). Of the ideas, 1 (stripes light or dark by the way the camera looks), 2 (the blades and patches of the grass) and 3 (the wear). No photographic texture.

**Decided**
- The option `grass_3d`, a choice of three, `plain` (no stripes) / `stripes` (8-2's stripes) / `realistic`, replaces 8-2's `grass_stripes_3d` (before a release: the old name is not read). `stripes` by default still (whether the realistic grass becomes the default, the user decides on the screen).
- The stripes' light and dark: a mower bends the blades its way, which look lighter seen along it and darker seen against it. The stripes (5.25 m as in 8-2) are taken as mowed along the field's y one way and back in turn, the even ones toward -z (light from the main cameras on the near side, as 8-2's stripes), the odd ones toward +z. The light and dark span ±6.5%, 35% of it whatever the way (the stripes show a little from behind the goals too). Seen straight down, only that part. From the other side, light and dark swap.
- The detail: a noise image made at the start (256 px, fractal noise of five scales, tiling both ways; no material). The patches (one tile in 23 m, ±6%) are painted into the ground image; the blades (one tile in 1.7 m, ±8%) are read by the shader only nearer than 30 m from the camera (fading from 15 m): far away the blades would average out anyway.
- The wear: both goal mouths (centered at x = ±49.5, 7 × 9 m), the penalty spots (2.5 m, 0.6) and the center (4 m, 0.5) toward the color of dry grass and soil (0.50, 0.50, 0.28), at most 0.6 of it, patchy by the patches, painted into the ground image. At first the colors were multiplied to look yellower, which hardly changed a saturated green; they are mixed instead.
- The stripes, the blades and the patches are over the lines too (real lines are painted on the grass, and show the mowed stripes).
- Why it is split so (measured): at first all of it was in the shader (the noise read twice, the five worn spots computed for every pixel), 1.0 to 1.3 ms more than the stripes on an integrated GPU. On that GPU, over the ground that covers most of the screen, each texture read takes about 0.25 to 0.5 ms. The wear computed near its spots only: +0.7 ms; the patches and the wear painted into the ground image and the blades read near the camera only: no difference from the stripes from the broadcast camera. Painting the ground image takes 15 to 23 ms with the rows in bands by up to 8 threads (about 80 ms by one, 160 ms as first written), once when the option or the colors change.

**Made**
- `GrassMode`, `grassModes()`, `grassModeName()`, `grassModeFromName()` (`camera_mode.h`). `grass_3d` of `Options`, and `--grass-3d` on the command line. Grass in Stadium of the settings dialog (three radio buttons with a summary, in place of the checkbox Grass Stripes).
- `src/view3d/grass_3d`: `noiseImage()`, `stripeShade()` and `bladesFade()` (as ground.frag), `wearAt()`, `paintGround()`, the constants. No GL.
- `GroundTexture::draw()`: with the realistic grass, no stripes, and the patches and the wear painted by `paintGround()`.
- The shaders `ground.vert` (passing the position in the world) and `ground.frag` (the stripes' light and dark, the blades near the camera). `Texture2D::upload()` takes repeating down and anisotropic filtering (none for the noise). The noise is on texture unit 2, and `GLFrame::end()` unbinds unit 2 too.
- `SceneContent::realistic_grass`, `View3DState::realistic_grass`.

**Tests**
- Test `grass_3d` (new): the noise (gray, a mean of 0.5 with a spread, the same for the same seed, as smooth across its edges as inside), the stripes' light and dark (from the near side the even ones light and the odd ones dark, on both sides of the halfway line, swapped from the other side, only the fixed part straight down and across, within the span from anywhere), the fading of the blades, the painting of the ground image (a goal mouth redder and less green, elsewhere lighter and darker by the patches but not redder, down to the bottom rows, the same image), the wear (most in the goal mouths, the penalty spots and the center, none elsewhere, the same at both ends, the edge of the bounds).
- Added to test `scene_3d`: a point seen from both sides of the pitch, the ratio of its lightness with the realistic grass to the plain ground's (the noise, the same at the same point, drops out): for an even stripe lighter from the near side and darker from the other, for an odd one the other way. Within one stripe straight down, the blades at 8 m, none at 28 m (nearly faded) and at 40 m.
- Fixed and added to tests `ground_texture` (no stripes with the realistic grass, a goal mouth redder, the lines still light, the key changing), `frame_builder_3d` (`realistic_grass` by the option), `config_dialog` (the three buttons, Stripes at first, Realistic and Plain chosen, Stripes back by Default).
- Every test was checked to fail with what it checks broken for a while (11 ways: not by the way the camera looks, the way mowed reversed (the shader and C++), no blades, the blades read far away without fading, no wear painted, no patches painted, the painting not called, Scene3D ignoring the option, the edge of the wear's bounds, the threads' last band not painted). At first removing the blades passed (the area looked at crossed a stripe's edge); it now looks within one stripe. Not fading the blades was not caught at first either; a check at 28 m was added.
- Measured (as in 8-1, 22 human bodies, the simple shadows, the sky and the stripes): from the broadcast camera, the stripes 3.0 to 3.1 ms, the realistic grass 3.0 to 3.1 ms; from the close-up camera 2.8 to 2.9 ms → 3.4 ms; from the player camera 2.1 to 2.3 ms → 2.6 to 2.7 ms (the blades read near).
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the clean builds and the tests pass (42 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (17) and autotools also pass.
- In a window not shown on the screen, frame 3,000 of the full game's log was drawn from the broadcast, close-up, player and goal cameras, with the stripes and the realistic grass, and looked at. From the player camera (looking along the pitch), the stripes are only the fixed part, faint (as real ones). The wear is subtle.

#### 2026-11-15: A compact settings dialog (`feature/3d-view`)

The 2 commits of 8-2b (124dd0f, c043f64) were pushed, and the CI passed. The user checked it on the screen and kept `stripes` as the grass's default.

**Decided (user)**
- The settings dialog had grown large: tidy it up and make it compact, the long notes in the tooltips.

**Made**
- The summaries under the rows of choices of the 3D and Player Camera tabs (of the one chosen; made in 6-6) are gone. Their texts were in the buttons' tooltips already.
- The notes under the groups (Close-up Camera, Guides, Players, Stadium, Player Sight) and the note at the top of the Player Camera tab went into the tooltips of the groups (and the tab).
- The parameter of `addChoices()` for the summaries and `noteLabel()`, which made the notes, are gone. The items and the options are as before.
- The size (drawn offscreen): from 500 × 714 to 408 × 465 (set by the tallest, the 3D tab). In 8-3 to 8-5, the 3D tab gets the items of the stands and the ball.
- The user pointed out: it is hard to see that an item has a tooltip (the web version underlines where a tooltip is). Fixed: `TooltipMarker` (`src/tooltip_marker`). After the dialog is made, labels, check boxes, radio buttons and group titles get an event filter, which, after a widget paints itself, draws a dotted line under its text if it has a tooltip (0.7 times as dark as the text, within the widget). The tooltip is looked at when painting, so one set later is marked too. Not on push buttons (the line would cross their frames), nor on labels of rich or wrapped text. A disabled item gets a faint line. All the tabs (the items with tooltips of the older tabs too). The web version follows the same policy (§0.2).

**Tests**
- Test `config_dialog`: "the summary is of the one chosen" of the Player Camera tab became "no line of the summary, its text in the button's tooltip". Added: the tooltips of the five groups whose notes moved are not empty, and a label with a tooltip is marked. Checked to fail with a group's tooltip removed, and without the marker installed.
- Test `tooltip_marker` (new): for a label, a check box, a radio button and a group title, with a tooltip only the strip right under the text changes (within its width, below the middle of its line, at most 2 px high), nothing without one, nothing on a push button, a tooltip set later, a disabled item, labels of rich and wrapped text. Checked to fail with what it checks broken (5 ways: the tooltip looked at only when installed, group titles left out, the line over the text, no line on a disabled item, not installed in the dialog). The filter installed on push buttons too is equivalent (no text place is given for them). At first, a label as high as its text got no line (it fell outside the widget); it is kept within. Qt5 needed `<QTextDocument>` for `Qt::mightBeRichText()`.
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the clean builds and the tests pass (43 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (18) and autotools also pass. The six tabs were drawn offscreen and looked at.

#### 2026-11-15: Step 8-3, stands (`feature/3d-view`)

The 2 commits of the settings dialog (c99bda8, 5c11f6b) were pushed, and the CI passed.

**Decided (user)**
- Others called the web version's stands "Claude-like". The stands are to have none of Claude's habits.

**What is "Claude-like" about the web version's stands (Claude's reading)**
- An abstract sign, not a shape made after real grounds: a staircase of one kind of step, gray seats and gray concrete.
- Regular and even: square people of one shape every 50 cm, a pattern of 8 m repeated (0.37 shifted a row), 8 saturated colors given out evenly, the team colors to 45% of each half of the pitch.

**The first build (two tiers and a roof; not taken in)**
- Built after the main stands of real grounds: along both touch lines, a lower tier (14 rows) from a low wall, hospitality boxes behind glass and a strip of LED boards, a steeper upper tier (12 rows), and a cantilevered roof. None behind the goals. The faces looked to the pitch, up, down and out from the ends only, see-through from outside and behind (the web version's idea). The painting of the crowd (below, "The crowd") was made then.
- That version is kept in the work tree's `git stash` ("8-3 stands: the two-tier version with the roof ...", with the plan and the tests). It is not made a choice (user; it would need the two-tier stands closed too and the ends filled). As a stash is easily lost, the 24 changed files themselves (`files/`), a patch for commit 5c11f6b (`stands-two-tier.patch`) and a README on bringing it back are kept outside the repository, in `../rcssmonitor-backup/stands-two-tier-2026-11-15/` next to this repository (2026-11-15; the patch applied to 5c11f6b gives the same files as the stash). It does not apply to the current branch: apply it to a worktree of 5c11f6b, or copy files such as `stands_3d` from it.

**The user's check and the rework**
- The user's points: (1) with the camera pulled back near the ground, the near stand is half see-through and in the way. (2) Only the sides have two-storied, luxurious stands, and nothing behind the goals feels wrong; the web version's plainness made stands only at the sides look right. (3) In the web version, being not see-through at all from behind made them look right too.
- The causes: (1) a camera in a single-sided stand, or low behind it, sees only some of its faces to the pitch (the treads facing up and so on), a scrap of a building. (3) The web version's stands are closed, 16 steps and a back wall, a wall seen from behind; rcssmonitor's two tiers had no faces turned out at the back and the ends, so they were see-through from behind.
- Decided (the compromise the user approved): stands of one tier (16 rows as the web version's, about 7.5 m high) at four places, along both touch lines and behind both goals (the corners open, as at old English grounds); closed solids, not see-through from behind or outside. A stand is not drawn only when the camera is inside it. The realistic painting of the crowd is kept.

**Decided (the rework)**
- Four stands (`Stands3D::stand()`): far (beyond the touch line, along +x), near (along -x), behind the left goal (along -z), behind the right goal (along +z). Each begins 2 m behind the boards and is as long as the boards (±58 m at the sides, ±39.5 m behind the goals). The image goes to the right seen from the pitch, so its text reads from anywhere. The sides end before the fronts of the ends; the corners open.
- The shape: from a front wall (1.2 m), 16 rows (0.85 m deep, 0.42 m high), a back wall (7.5 m high, facing out), and walls at both ends (stepped as the rows, facing out). The treads face up, the risers and the front wall the pitch, the back wall and the ends out: a closed solid, so from anywhere its back faces are culled, and from outside its walls show (as the web version's). Stairs every 14 m (their nosings a faded yellow); vomitories in rows 5 to 7 at ±7 m and ±35 m (those within the stand's length). No roof, boxes nor LED strip.
- A camera inside (`insideStand()`): within a stand's length and depth, and lower than the row over that depth plus 0.5 m (for the near plane). Only that stand is not drawn (a close-up camera far and low near a touch line, for one). Seen from inside a closed solid, most faces are back faces and culled, but the treads of the lower rows show from above, a scrap.
- The crowd: the whole length of a stand in one image (no pattern repeating). Mostly the plain clothes of a real crowd (black, charcoal, navy, denim, gray, white, beige, brown, olive, and a little red, maroon and mustard, by weights). The fans gather toward their team's end (the left team's share 1 / (1 + exp(x / 14 m))): 45% at the sides (65% toward the ends), 70% behind a goal, nearly all the team that keeps it. 70% of the fans wear the team color (its brightness a little varied), 15% a dark shade of it. The sides are fuller in the middle (93%) than toward the corners (43%); behind a goal 88%, 63% at the ends; the back rows a little emptier. Empty seats show their blue. People are seated and seen from the front (the head with its corners cut round, hair on 6 in 10, the neck, the shoulders, the arms in the shade, the hands on the lap, the thighs, two legs and the shoes), of five skin colors.
- The image: one for each stand (2784 × 432 px at the sides, 1896 × 432 px behind the goals; 24 px a meter, 24 px a row). A band for each row with the riser (the bodies and heads of the seated) over the tread (the legs and the seat pans), then the front wall and concrete (the back and end walls). Drawn again only when the team colors or names change. At first 16 px a meter, which made people look like blocky pixel art from a close-up camera; 24 px, with the shapes refined. Light treads made gray horizontal stripes from the broadcast camera; they are darkened as the shade of the rows. The stairs are darker concrete too.
- The light: as the players' (all around, the sky, the sun's Lambert). No shadows cast or received.
- The option `stands_3d` (on by default; in the group `View3D`) and `--stands-3d` on the command line. Stands in Stadium of the settings dialog.

**Made**
- `src/view3d/stands_3d`: the constants of the sizes, `Stand`, `stand()`, `depth()`, `top()`, `mesh()` (the world's positions, the normals, the places in the image), `insideStand()`, `imageWidth()`, `imageHeight()`, `rowTop()`, `image()`. No GL.
- The shaders `stand.vert`, `stand.frag`. `Scene3D::setStands()` (a stand at a time), a pass of the stands (after the panels, before the sky; a stand with the camera inside skipped).
- `SceneContent::stands`, `View3DState::stands`. `FieldView3D` draws the four images again when the team colors or names change.
- `stands_3d` of `Options`, Stands of `ConfigDialog`.

**Tests**
- Test `stands_3d` (new): the four stands' directions, places and lengths, the corners open, 7.5 m high. The triangles (of an area, counterclockwise seen from where they face, the normals up, to the pitch, out and out from the ends only, the ends facing out from the middle, within the stand, their places in the image within 0 to 1), the numbers of treads and risers, the back wall (facing out), the number of end faces. A camera inside (under the rows, above, in front, behind, beyond an end, another stand, the goal camera above). The images (their sizes, the same image, at the sides the fans more than 3 times as many toward their team's third, behind a goal the team that keeps it more than 10 times, people in plain clothes, no one in the aisles, the stairs light concrete in every row, each stand's image its own).
- Added to test `scene_3d`: the far stand drawn seen from the pitch; the same in every frame; from inside the near stand (under its rows), a lower row's tread's pixel as without the stands; from low behind the near stand, its back wall (concrete) seen.
- Added to tests `frame_builder_3d` (`stands` by the option, `clear()`) and `config_dialog` (the tooltip of Stands, on at first, the option changed by a click, back by Default).
- Every test was checked to fail with what it checks broken for a while (7 ways: no back wall, drawn with the camera inside, always inside, the fans' sides reversed, the stairs not painted, the end faces turned in, the depth of inside widened). At first, drawing with the camera inside and turning the end faces in passed. For the first, seen from inside a closed solid most faces are culled, and nothing showed from the test's place; the camera now looks down at a lower row's tread. For the second, a check that the end faces face out from the middle was added.
- Measured (as in 8-1, 22 human bodies, the simple shadows, the sky and the stripes): GPU from the broadcast camera 3.0 ms without the stands, 3.2 to 3.4 ms with them; from the player camera 2.2 to 2.3 ms and 2.3 to 2.4 ms; from a goal camera 3.0 ms and 3.3 ms. Drawing the four images takes 4 to 6 ms (only when the team colors or names change).
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the clean builds and the tests pass (44 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (18) and autotools also pass.
- In a window not shown on the screen, frame 3,000 of the full game's log was drawn from the broadcast, close-up, player, goal and whole-pitch cameras, and from a far, low close-up camera (40 m, 5° up), and looked at.

#### 2026-11-15: Step 8-4, the ball's pattern and rolling (`feature/3d-view`)

The two commits of the stands (adf3d23, 5e3dcb9) were pushed, and CI passed. The record of the backup of the two-tier stands (a7035a5) was only committed (user).

**Decided**
- As in the web version (`ball.ts`, §4.3): twelve dark pentagons at the corners of an icosahedron (their corners a third of the way to the neighbors, about 21°), 0.12 times the ball's color. The shader finds the pentagons at each pixel from the direction on the ball, so that their edges stay sharp however near the camera is (smoothed over a pixel). Lit as the players.
- Rolling: from the frames of the log alone (§5.2). The table of the ball's turns (`BallSpin`) adds up, from the first frame, how far it rolled at each frame (the distance moved on the ground over the radius, about up × the way), found once and kept. At most 0.8π a cycle (so that a fast ball does not seem to turn back at 60 fps). Between two frames, by the fraction. Unlike the web version, it rolls by the rule of the interpolation: not over a play mode change, nor over a move farther than `Interpolate::MAX_BALL_STEP` (4 m; a placement) (the web version: 5 m, without the play mode). The ball turns as far as it is drawn moving. When a live game drops its oldest frames, the turns of the frames left stay. Another log or another radius starts again.
- With a height (`ball.z`) too, it rolls by the move on the ground (to be looked at again when the format of the 2.5D server is decided).
- The option `ball_pattern_3d` (on by default; group `View3D`) and the command line `--ball-pattern-3d`. The 3D tab of the settings dialog has a new group Ball between Players and Stadium, with Pattern (8-5 adds Ring, Trail and so on). Without the pattern the turn is still found (only not seen).
- The ball's sphere is divided more finely than the heads (32 × 24; its outline is near the close cameras).

**Made**
- `src/view3d/ball_3d`: `Ball3D::pentagons()`, `patternAt()` (as ball.frag), `roll()`, `rolls()`, `BallSpin` (`update()`, `turnAt()`, `clear()`). No GL.
- Shaders `ball.vert` and `ball.frag`. `ShaderProgram::setArray()` (a uniform array of vec3). `Scene3D`: the ball's pass within the pass of the lit solids, with the finer sphere `M_ball`.
- The argument `ball_turn` of `SceneBuilder::addShow()`. `SceneContent::ball_pattern`, `View3DState::ball_pattern` and `ball_turn`. `FrameBuilder3D` keeps the table and gives the frame's turn to the ball.
- `ball_pattern_3d` of `Options`, Ball and Pattern of `ConfigDialog`.

**Tests**
- Test `ball_3d` (new): the pentagons (of length 1, five neighbors each 63.4° away, the corners toward the neighbors, a third of the way), the pattern (the centers, out to PENTAGON toward a corner, the five corners, out to cos 36° times as far toward the middle of an edge, white halfway to a neighbor, 27 to 33% of the ball dark), rolling (along the field's x the top goes toward +x, along y toward +z, the angle the distance over the radius with its limit and the fraction, the point on the ground does not slide, no turn without a move), when it rolls (the play mode, a placement), the table (adding up frame by frame, the fraction, without a next frame, beyond the end, the same frame by frame, no turn over a placement or a play mode change), live (the turns left the same after the oldest are dropped and after more frames, other frames and another radius start again, `clear()`).
- Added to test `scene_3d`: a large ball drawn from straight above without perspective; whether each pixel of the ball (within 0.85 of the radius) is dark agrees with `patternAt()` at the direction turned back by the roll for more than 97% (not turned and turned), 15 to 50% dark, nothing dark without the pattern, the turned ball not agreeing with the pattern not turned, the same in every frame.
- Added to tests `scene_builder` (the ball's matrix turned by the turn given, of the same radius), `frame_builder_3d` (no turn before the kick, turning on after it, the turn of the ball's matrix, the same turn drawn again, after other frames, by another builder, and live with frames coming later, `ball_pattern` by the option, `clear()`) and `config_dialog` (the tooltips of the group Ball and of Pattern, on at first, the option changed by a click, back by Default).
- Every test was checked to fail with what it checks broken for a while (16 ways: the shader not using the turned direction, no pattern, the shader's distance of the edges, the CPU's, the axis of rolling reversed, no limit, rolling over a play mode change, no fraction, starting again when the oldest are dropped, a change of the radius ignored, the ball's matrix not turned, FrameBuilder3D not turning between frames, the option ignored, the dialog setting another option, Default not setting it back, the corners toward the farthest corner). The last one does not show in the drawing (the CPU and the shader use the same values), but `ball_3d` catches it.
- Measured (as in 8-1, 22 human bodies, the simple shadows, the sky, the stripes and the stands): GPU from the broadcast camera 3.3 to 3.4 ms without the pattern and 3.3 to 3.4 ms with it, from the ball's close-up camera 3.5 to 3.8 ms and 3.5 to 3.9 ms (no difference to measure). The table of the turns takes 0.6 ms for the full game's log (6,209 frames) and 0.8 ms for 8,406 frames (once, at the first drawing after a log is read); a frame's turn about 0.1 µs.
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the clean builds and the tests pass (45 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (18) and autotools also pass.
- In a window not shown on the screen, frames 3,000 to 3,002 of the full game's log were drawn from the ball's close-up camera (3 m, 15° up); the pattern turned from frame to frame as the ball rolled.

#### 2026-11-15: Step 8-5, the ball's ring and trail, and the measurement with everything on (`feature/3d-view`)

The two commits of 8-4 (2bf13c2, ce69bc7) were pushed with the record of the backup of the two-tier stands (a7035a5), and CI passed.

**Decided**
- As in the web version (`ball-aids.ts`, §4.3):
  - The ring: on the ground around the ball, 0.7 m in radius (the default kickable margin; 0.1 to 5 m). Its line 0.06 m or 2 pixels on the screen, whichever is wider (at most half the radius), 0.9 opaque, over a fill 0.12 opaque inside. 0.028 m above the ground (under the ball's shadow), over the circles under the players and their rings. A square of 2 m on the ground, on which the shader draws the line and the fill by the distance from the center (the edges smoothed over a pixel).
  - The trail: a ribbon through the ball's centers of the last cycles (5 by default; 1 to 30). Exactly that many cycles back from the time drawn (the frame and the fraction), the last point part of the way between two frames. The ribbon faces the camera (across the way and the direction to the eye), its head 3 times the ball's radius or 6 pixels on the screen wide, whichever is wider, narrower (the fraction left to the power of 0.6) and fainter (0.75 times the fraction left) the older, dense along its middle and clear at its edges. Drawn after everything else translucent.
- The trail is from the frames of the log alone (§5.2). Unlike the web version, it breaks by the rule of the interpolation, as the rolling of 8-4 (at a play mode change and at a placement farther than 4 m; the web version: 5 m, without the play mode).
- The widths of the ring and the trail depend on the camera, so they are added after the camera is known, as the labels, by `FrameBuilder3D::addBallAids()`. Where the ball is not drawn (Ball of Show off, not seen from the player camera), neither is drawn. The screen's height is in logical pixels.
- The options `ball_ring_3d`, `ball_ring_radius_3d`, `ball_trail_3d` and `ball_trail_cycles_3d` (group `View3D`) and the command line by the same names. Out of the ranges, not read; the setters bring them within. Ring, Trail and the fields Ring Radius and Trail Cycles in Ball of the settings dialog; the fields dim while the ring or the trail is off. The dialog is now 408 × 557 (the Ball group about 90 pixels taller).

- `SceneContent::ball_rings`, `ball_ring_radius`, `ball_ring_width`, `ball_trail`. `FrameBuilder3D::addBallAids()`, `View3DState::ball_ring`, `ball_ring_width`, `ball_trail`. `FieldView3D` calls it after the labels.

- Added to test `scene_3d`: from straight above without perspective, the ring's line light, its inside faint, nothing outside (three ways); the trail light along its middle, fainter to its edges and to its tail, nothing beside or ahead of it; the same in every frame. The first frame after switching to the top camera the same as the next one, and the first after switching back to the broadcast camera as before (§11: 4; frames built by FrameBuilder3D with the figures, the detailed shadows, the realistic grass, the ring and the trail).
- Added to tests `frame_builder_3d` (the ring and the trail by the options; the ring under the ball, its line 0.06 m near and wider far away; the number of the trail's points and its head at the ball; the same trail however reached; the cycles and the radius by the options; none when off; none without the ball drawn; the options' ranges) and `config_dialog` (the tooltips, the tooltip of Default, the values at first, the fields setting the options, the fields dimmed while off, back by Default).

The ranges of the setters were first missed (the dialog's fields keep them), so a check of the options' ranges was added. The test of switching to the top camera has nothing to break in this build (Scene3D sends all of the content at each drawing, so the problem of the web version, when the data reach the GPU, cannot happen).

#### 2026-11-15: The tool bar as the web version's, and the FPS over the 3D view (`feature/3d-view`)

The two commits of 8-5 (87e4773, 05d4c94) and the commit making the constants of `Options` constexpr (5a397f8) were pushed, and CI passed. The user checked 8-5 on the screen.

**Decided (user)**
- The tool bar as the web version's: buttons to open a file, connect, disconnect and kick off, loop playback, the cycle and the frame number, a field to go to a cycle, and the time per frame while playing. Not about 3D, but on `feature/3d-view`: the tool bar has the selector of the 3D cameras, and another branch would conflict with it.
- Only the new buttons get icons of the web version's shapes (the XPM of the present buttons stay).
- An option showing the FPS over the 3D view, off by default.
- These are part of Phase 8 too, in one commit.
- (After checking on the screen) With the slider's least width, the window had to be much wider to show the parts from the cycle label on. Show them as far as possible; two rows as the web version's are fine.

**Made**
- `src/toolbar_icons`: `drawnIcon()`, `drawnIconImage()`. The web version's 16 × 16 SVG icons (`icons.ts`) drawn with QPainter in the same shapes (the same stroke widths, joins and arc directions). Drawn at 16, 24, 32 and 48 pixels into a QIcon, sharp at any screen scale. In the text color of the palette, drawn again when the palette changes (as the present XPM icons).
- The tool bar: first Open, Connect, Disconnect and Kick Off, then after a gap the present buttons from Live, Loop after the next score, and after the slider "cycle (frame / frames)" (`1746.3` in a stopped time), the cycle field and the time per frame (`100 ms`). In the web version's order.
  - Kick Off can be pressed only while connected, as Disconnect (it could be pressed always and did nothing when not connected).
  - Loop switches `auto_loop_mode`, which could be set only in the settings file and on the command line (`Options::setAutoLoopMode()` added). Also in the Monitor menu.
  - The cycle field: digits only; with Enter, to the frame of the cycle, or the first one after it (as the web version and the present `LogPlayer::goToCycle()`). A cycle after the last frame turns the field red without going (typing takes it off). After going, the field is cleared and the keys go back to the view; Esc too.
  - The time per frame: while playing, the interval made slower or faster; stopped, the option's interval (`LogPlayer::interval()` added).
  - The slider's least width from 600 to 200 pixels, taking the rest of the width. With 600 the new fields did not fit in the window.
  - The tool bar is split in two: the buttons (LogPlayer) and the slider with the parts after it (Position). When the window is too narrow for both, Position goes to a second row below the buttons (a tool bar break), and back to one row when it is wide enough (`MainWindow::updateToolBarRows()`: when the window is resized, when the buttons' width changes with the 3D view, when the tool bars are shown). It compares the buttons' wanted width and the least width with all of Position's parts shown (the sum of its parts' least widths). Position's `minimumSizeHint()` is a small value (230 pixels) for hiding the parts that do not fit behind the "»" menu; compared with it at first, it never went to two rows. Only for the two in one area (after the user moves a tool bar, it is left alone). View > Tool Bar shows and hides both.
- `Options::setGameLogFile()` (for the tests: the game log opened at the start).
- `src/view3d/fps_meter` (`FpsMeter`): the frames of the last second, and the GPU's time of a frame (the timer queries, a few frames late). The 3D view draws only while something moves, and Qt 6 stops at the screen's rate (60 fps), so the frames alone do not tell the room; the GPU's time with them ("60 fps · GPU 5.6 ms"). "- fps" with fewer than two frames in the second, and in the last frame before the view stops drawing (which stays on the screen).
- The option `fps_3d` (off by default; group `View3D`) and the command line `--fps-3d`; FPS in Guides of the 3D tab of the settings dialog. `FieldView3D` takes the GPU's times with `RCSSMONITOR_3D_TIMING` or `fps_3d`, and writes it white on translucent black at the bottom right (over the minimap when it is shown).

**Tests**

- The two rows in test `toolbar` (one row at 1600 wide, two at 700 with the last part (the time per frame) in the window, back to one when wide, both shown and hidden by View > Tool Bar).
- Test `fps_meter` (new): 60 fps and 30 fps, the frames leaving the window, still, the last frame, the GPU's time (the last kept when none came), the text.
- Added to test `config_dialog`: the tooltip of FPS, off at first, the option changed by a click, back by Default, the tooltip of Default.
- Every test was checked to fail with what it checks broken for a while (16 ways: the stopped time, the frame number from 1, no red after the last, Loop not setting the option, Kick Off pressable at first, not drawn again with the palette, the arc's direction, the icons not scaled, Esc, the red not taken off by typing, the FPS's last frame, the FPS's window, no GPU time, the dialog, Default, the time per frame). And the two rows in 5 ways (never two rows, always two, the parts' widths not counted, not looked at again on resizing, View > Tool Bar only one of them). The test of the buttons' order shows the tool bar, hidden by default.

#### 2026-11-15: Step 8-6, the ball flying in the air, virtually (`feature/3d-view`)

The two commits of the tool bar and the FPS display (aff2db3, 54ab962) were pushed, and CI passed.

**Decided (user)**
- Today's rcssserver is 2D and the ball never flies, but in log replay at least, the ball should fly in the air virtually: from the initial speed of a kick and the time and distance to the next touch, a decoration making the segment fly, bounce and so on. Reference: Hatakeyama et al., "Log visualization system for soccer analysis" (JSAI SIG-SAI-047-05, 2024).
- Step 8-6 of Phase 8. The option `ball_flight_3d` off by default (a decoration guessing what the log does not have: only when the user chooses it).
- The paper's formulation (the highest point after n bounces under a threshold, $h_n = (v_0 e^n)^2/2g \le h_\theta$, and the time to it within the time to the next kick, $t_{0\sim n} = \frac{2v_0}{g}\cdot\frac{1-e^n}{1-e} \le t_{pass}$) checks whether an initial speed meets the conditions; it decides neither whether to loft nor a single initial speed. Made by the following method, which Claude proposed.

**Decided (the method)**
- The way on the ground is the log's; only the height is added (no gap with the 2D view and the overlays).
- The segments: the log is cut at the moments of the touches (the frame before the bit of a kick, a tackle or a catch, as the figures' actions), where the ball hit a player (the frame of the player's `BALL_COLLIDE` bit; added after the user's remark below), at a change of the ball's velocity its decay does not explain (a post; larger than the jitter of `ball_rand`), at a play mode change and at a placement (a jump farther than 4 m). But not at a set piece's kick, which changes the play mode itself (corner_kick to play_on). A tackle's bit stays, so only its start is a touch.
- The reasons to loft (rcssserver slows the ball on the ground and in the air alike, so the horizontal move does not tell; decided from the cues of the scenes where a real player lofts the ball): a kick at least 1.5 m/cycle fast going at least 10 m to the next touch, and one of:
  - a set piece: a corner kick, a goal kick of 20 m or more;
  - a cross: from a wing (|y| ≥ 18, x ≥ 25 toward the goal attacked) into the penalty area;
  - a shot: from 12 m or more, to the line of the goal attacked (ending with a play mode change or a catch, within 4 m of the posts); only a little;
  - opponents on the pass's line: an opponent within 2.5 m of the ball's way that did not touch it (from 3 cycles after the kick to 3 before the next touch); it went over;
  - a long ball: 20 m or more. In four full games, the fast kicks (1.5 m/cycle or more) go 11 to 14 m in the median, and 40 to 59 a game (about a tenth) 20 m or more.
- The shape of the height: a flight under gravity (9.8 m/s², a cycle 0.1 s), the bounces (restitution 0.6: going up 0.6 times as fast), rolling below a bounce of 2 cm; the paper's geometric series of the bounces.
- The upward speed: of those meeting the constraints below, the one whose highest point is nearest the one preferred for the segment (1,500 steps from 0 to the speed of a 12 m top; coarse steps miss a narrow window of speeds). With none, on the ground.
  - At the next touch, no higher than the toucher reaches: a foot 0.5 m, a tackle 0.3 m, a goalkeeper's catch 2.3 m, a body 1.8 m. Everyone kicks with a foot in a 2D log: no kick of a ball 2 m up.
  - Where it crosses the goal line between the posts, the ball's top under the crossbar (2.44 m) (a goal in the 2D server).
  - At an opponent on the pass's line, over its head (2.0 m or more).
  - The preferred highest point: a tenth of the distance (1.5 to 8 m); 6% for a shot (0.8 to 2 m); 0.3 m over a head when only for the opponents on the line.
- Continuity: a segment starts at the height the one before ended at (a volley). The paper starts each from the ground, so a ball kicked back in the air jumps to the ground. From the ground after a play mode change or a placement. When a segment ends at a placement (a jump), its distance is up to the frame before (not to the place it was put).
- From the frames of the log alone, once per segment (§5.2): the same height paused, stepped back or jumped to. Between two frames, the height at the time of the fraction (on the arc; without the fraction where the interpolation does not move the ball). 1 to 2 ms for a full game.
- Not live (the next touch is not known yet). With heights in the log (a 2.5D server), those.
- The height works wherever the 3D view uses one: the ball drawn, its shadow right below (fainter with height), the trail (an arc: its points at the virtual heights), the goalkeeper's catch (crouching or the hands up by the height at the catch).
- The close-up camera of the ball still looks at the ball's place on the ground (a high ball goes up in the view). The IK of the kicking foot stays on the ground (at most 0.5 m at the receiver).
- The option `ball_flight_3d` (off by default; group `View3D`) and the command line `--ball-flight-3d`. Flight in Ball of the 3D tab of the settings dialog.

**Made**
- `src/view3d/ball_flight_3d` (`BallFlight`): `update()`, `clear()`, `active()`, `segments()`, `heightAt()`, `heightAtFrame()`, `height()` (a segment's height). All constants in the header. No GL.
- `FrameBuilder3D`: `setLive()`, `flight()`. With the option and not live, it makes the table and puts the height in the interpolated show's ball. `View3DState::ball_flight`, `ball_height`. `FieldView3D` tells whether live.
- An argument for the virtual heights (`const BallFlight *`) of `FigureActionLog::read()` and `BallAids3D::trailPoints()`.
- `ball_flight_3d` of `Options`, Flight of `ConfigDialog`.

**Tests**
- Test `ball_flight_3d` (new): the shape (the flight, its top, the bounces' heights and lengths, rolling, never under the ground, dropped from a height), a long ball (lofted, its top near the preferred, under 0.5 m at the receiver, the next segment from there, continuous on the arc between frames, under 2.3 m for a catch), on the ground (a short pass, a slow kick, a tackle's touch, over an opponent without room to come down to the receiver), opponents on the line (over the head; not for a teammate), set pieces and crosses (a corner kick not cut, a cross, the opposite way for the right team), shots (a little in the air, under the crossbar, from the ground after a placement, a long ball into the own goal under the crossbar too, a shot over a defender just before the goal that cannot pass under the bar stays on the ground), a long ball ending at a placement as high as the way before it asks, the cuts (a play mode change, a jump, one touch for a tackle's bit that stays, a body), the frames (the same not made again, more made again, the log's heights used, empty), the readers (the catch at the virtual height, the trail's points at the virtual heights).
- Added to tests `frame_builder_3d` (on the ground without the option, in the air with it and the ball drawn at the height, on the ground before the kick, the same however reached, on the ground live) and `config_dialog` (the tooltip of Flight, off at first, the option changed by a click, back by Default).
- Every test was checked to fail with what it checks broken for a while (23 ways: no bounce, gravity's formula, the receiver's reach, the crossbar, over the head, teammates counted as opponents, cutting at a set piece's kick, the continuity, the height kept after a placement, no collision, the cross's direction, no long ball, the log's heights ignored, not made again with more frames, the fraction between frames, the catch's height, the trail's height, live, the option, the dialog, a tackle's bit that stays, no shot, the distance before a placement). The crossbar, the tackle's bit that stays and the shot were missed at first (no scene where the crossbar binds or a tackle's bit stays, and the test of shots passed with long balls too); a scene for each was added.
- Found while making it: a set piece's kick was cut at its play mode change as a placement (no corner or goal kick flew in the full games); a segment ending at a placement measured its distance to the place it was put; the test logs had players near the ball's way.
- Four full games: 38 to 62 kicks a game lofted (3 to 5% of the kicks); highest points 1.8 to 2.3 m on average, 3 to 7 m at most; the table made in 1 to 2 ms.
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the clean builds and the tests pass (49 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) and autotools also pass.
- In a window not shown on the screen, a long ball (the broadcast camera and the ball's close-up) and a cross (the ball's close-up) of the full games were drawn: the ball flies on an arc, the trail arcs too, and the shadow and the ring stay on the ground.

**The user's check and the change (8-6)**
- The user checked it on the screen: the flight is fine, but the lofted balls are almost all taken directly, and they rarely bounce halfway. The shorter moves should go up a little too, and the ones of 20 m or more should mix in bounces.
- The cause: choosing the highest point near the one for the distance, under 0.5 m at the receiver, gave flights that land first near the receiver. Of the lofted kicks, under 40% landed more than a cycle before the receiver.
- Changed:
  - Of the long ones (long balls, set pieces, crosses), 60% (`BOUNCE_SHARE`) are chosen by their first landing near 50 to 75% (`MIN_LANDING` to `MAX_LANDING`) of the segment's time (bouncing on to the receiver); the rest by their highest point as before (direct). The constraint at the receiver stays, so where landing at the preferred time would bounce too high, the nearest time.
  - A fast kick without a reason to loft (1.2 m/cycle or more, 6 m or more) goes a little up (`Loft::Lift`; highest point 0.2 to 0.7 m), lands and bounces low. It ignores the opponents on the line (it passes low).
  - Which, what time and what height vary from kick to kick by its time and its kicker (`BallFlight::variety()`: 0 to 1 by the mixing of MurmurHash3's finalizer). Not at random: the same paused or rewound.
- Four full games: 37 to 61 long ones a game, 26 to 37 of them bouncing halfway and 7 to 25 direct; 182 to 430 passes a game going up a little (0.45 m high on average). About half of the lofted ones land before the receiver. The table in 3 to 6 ms.
- Tests: added to test `ball_flight_3d`: the same kick the same value and each kick its own; the long ones split into bouncing and direct by the time (the bouncing at the preferred time, or before it under the receiver's constraint, and up again; the direct no higher than the preferred); the lift of a short pass (its top the kick's value). The test on the ground uses a slow short pass now, and the scene with a teammate goes up a little. Checked to fail with what is checked broken (8 ways: no bounce, the landing time unused, a fixed lift, a constant variety, slow kicks lifted, kicks without a reason not lifted (two places), bouncing for the opponents on the line too).
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the clean builds and the tests pass (49 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) and autotools also pass. A long ball bouncing halfway was drawn from a low close-up in a window not shown on the screen.

**The user's remark and the change (8-6, collisions)**
- The user's remark: whether the ball was touched does not include the ball colliding with a player.
- Checked: the touches guessed collisions only from changes of the ball's velocity its decay does not explain, without the players' state bits. The log has the bit `BALL_COLLIDE` for a player that collided with the ball. A cycle of rcssserver goes: the commands (kicks...), moving the ball and the players, resolving the collisions (`Stadium::collisions()`: `BALL_TO_PLAYER | BALL_COLLIDE` for a player overlapping the ball, telling the referees it touched the ball), writing the log, clearing the states. A kick's bit tells it kicked before the move into its frame, so the moment is the frame before the bit; a collision's bit tells it hit during the move into its frame, the ball there already back from the body, so the moment is the frame of the bit itself.
- Changed:
  - Segments are cut at a frame with a player's `BALL_COLLIDE` bit (the moment: that frame). When the same frame is a kick's (or another touch's) moment, it is a kick (the ball hit the receiver, which kicked it at once). A ball hitting a post has no bit, so the changes of the velocity are still looked for.
  - The highest at a collision is a body's (1.8 m), but when a kick, a tackle or a catch follows within 3 cycles (`TRAP_CYCLES`), that touch's (0.5 m for a foot): a trap, not a ball stopped at the chest and kicked 0.1 s later. Each segment has its highest at the end (`Segment::reach`), found when the segments are made.
- Four full games: 151 to 253 frames a game with a collision bit, most in the same cycle as the receiver's kick (a kick); 50 to 111 segments a game cut at a collision (25 to 46 from the velocity before).
- Tests: added to test `ball_flight_3d`: cut at the frame of a collision bit, the next from there (even with no sign in the ball's move); a kick soon after a collision makes a foot's reach the highest, a kick long after a body's; a kick at the moment of a collision is a kick. Checked to fail with what is checked broken (4 ways: the bit ignored, a collision counted before a kick, the kick soon after ignored, always soon after).
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the clean builds and the tests pass (49 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) and autotools also pass.

### Phase 9

#### 2026-11-15: The plan of Phase 9

The record of the end of Phase 8 (8d46ee7) was pushed.

**What the present code lacks (found)**
- Models are glTF only (cgltf), without textures. The bundled bodies are painted in their materials' colors, but Mixamo and VRM models have their colors in textures.
- The bone roles are mapped for Quaternius's skeleton only (`quaterniusBoneNames()`). None for Mixamo (`mixamorig:Hips` and so on), VRM (its file has the roles) or BVH.
- Every model's rest pose is taken as a T-pose. No correction bringing an A-pose model to a T-pose.
- The clips are only those in the bundled Mannequin's glTF. No clips in other files, no removal of the root motion, no FBX or BVH.
- The actions (kicks, tackles and so on) are poses made in code only, with no way to play clips for them. Walking and running have forward clips only.
- The figure model is a fixed choice of three (Simple, Mannequin, Human), with no place to choose a pack or show its credits.
- The work machine has no Blender. The test assets (glTF, BVH, ASCII FBX) are made small by scripts and kept in `test/data`. Mixamo's assets cannot be distributed, so the user checks the real ones. → At the start of 9-1, the user installed Blender 5.3.0. The test assets can be made by Blender's scripts too.

**Decided (user)**
- The steps as in Phase 9's work above (9-1 to 9-6). glTF and VRM first (the base of drawing and bone mapping without a new dependency), FBX after (only its reading on that base).
- The bundled Mannequin and human bodies are written as packs (what the format lacks (kinds of bodies, hairstyles, the uniform's painting) shows early). They look and move the same, checked by the tests.
- Walking and running clips with directions are made from 100STYLE (CC BY 4.0; walking and running forward, backward and sideways, BVH) and bundled (also checking the BVH reading), with the credits shown.

#### 2026-11-15: Step 9-1, the base of the packs (`feature/3d-view`)

The plan of Phase 9 is committed with 9-1.

**Decided**

The bundled packs are named `mannequin` and `human`, so the values of the settings before are read as they are. The name of a pack that is not there is read too (the pack may be put later); it is drawn as cylinders, with the reason told. The option `figure_folder_3d` (a folder of packs of the user's choice) and `--figure-folder-3d` on the command line were added. The Default button does not set the folder back (it is not a look).

As many numbers are drawn, in the same order as before (the sex, the hairstyle, the first extra, the skin, the hair color, the extras from the second). With men and women mixed, a woman's body by half the chance, else a man's, one of them by the same number stretched. A body of no sex goes with both. Without a body of a sex, any body.

A pack that cannot be read is marked "(cannot be read)" and cannot be chosen, the reason in its tooltip. A pack that could not be drawn is marked "(cannot be drawn)" and can be chosen again (tried again). A name of no pack is "(not found)". Under the list, the credits in short (the authors and the licenses), in full in the tooltip (the titles and the URLs); if something is wrong, the reason in red instead. The menu of the Packs button beside the list: choose a folder, no folder, open the user's folder of packs (made if missing), look for the packs again. The dialog looks for the packs each time it shows. Bodies can be chosen only for a pack with men's and women's bodies.

The cause: the line was put as a string elided to a width decided from the list's and the button's widths, which was too narrow with the CI's fonts (it fitted with the fonts here).
