#### 2026-11-15: Step 9-2, glTF and VRM models (`feature/3d-view`)

The fix of 9-1's CI (bfec9e9) was pushed, and the CI passed.

**Decided**
- Models: glTF 2.0 .glb, .gltf (the buffers and images in files beside it or inside it; data URIs too), .vrm (VRM 0.x and 1.0). cgltf reads the files through Qt (`QFile`), so those in the resources can be read too. All the meshes are read: the skinned ones with one list of joints of the skins joined (a node with the same inverse bind matrix is one joint), and one without a skin fixed to its node (a hat and so on). A mesh without normals gets smooth ones. KHR_texture_transform is read (the texture coordinates scaled, turned and moved, and another set). If a file requires an extension that is not read (a compression of the meshes or textures: KHR_draco_mesh_compression, EXT_meshopt_compression, KHR_texture_basisu, EXT_texture_webp and so on), the extension's name is the reason it cannot be read, rather than drawing it wrong. KHR_mesh_quantization (read as floats), the materials' extensions (the base color read, the rest of the shading not used), the lights and VRM are read even when required.
- Materials: the base color and the base color texture (metallic-roughness, or else the diffuse of the older specular-glossiness), alphaMode (MASK: not drawn where more transparent than alphaCutoff, and casting no shadow there; BLEND: drawn as a mask at 0.5, as the figures are not sorted from far to near), doubleSided (drawn from behind too, the back lit with its normal turned over). A texture larger than 1024 px is made smaller.
- Paints (`paint`): `model` (the material's color times the texture), `team` and `uniform` (only the texture's transparency used), `tint` (new: the player's color with the texture's light and dark), `skin` (new: the skin color with the texture's light and dark), `hair` (the hairstyles and the extras too; the texture's light and dark). The light and dark are the texture's brightness divided by its mean (not making the color as a whole darker or lighter; the mean of the parts not transparent, found on the image made 64 px).
- Skeletons (`skeleton`): the bones' roles by VRM's names. The presets `quaternius`, `mixamo`, `vroid`, `vrm` (the roles a VRM file tells), or the pack's map (an object from the roles to the bones' names). A preset's names may follow a prefix ending with `:` or `_` (`mixamorig:Hips`). Without it, VRM's roles, or else the first preset that fits.
- The rest pose: an arm more than 10° (`ARM_POSE_TOLERANCE`) off straight out sideways (an A pose) is taken as a T pose, by a turn that puts the upper arm straight out, and one more of the lower arm if that is not enough (`HumanoidRig::restTurn()`; the roles below follow the correction above). The roles' turns are told from the corrected directions, so a motion of a model in a T pose can be put on a model in an A pose. The facing is found from the roots of the two legs; one more than 2° off +z is turned to face +z (`GltfModel::turnAbout()`: a new root node, turned; VRM 0.x faces -z). Both by themselves, not written in `figure.json`. A model without a correction (the bundled ones) keeps its rest to the last bit (Qt's product of quaternions is not exact even by the identity, so the identity is not multiplied).
- Drawing: only the parts with a texture or a transparency are drawn with a variant of the shader (`#define TEXTURED`). The others are drawn with the same shader as before. At first one shader told a texture by a uniform, and the GPU was about 0.3 ms slower with the bundled 22 human bodies. The texture coordinates are in a buffer of their own, apart from the vertices (vertices of 92 bytes instead of 84 made the GPU about 0.15 ms slower from the close-up camera, even with the human bodies of no texture; the cause was confirmed by padding the vertices of the code before by 8 bytes, which made it as much slower). In the shadow map, the parts with a transparency are drawn with a shader that leaves out the transparent parts of a mask (`skinned_depth.frag`).
- The bundled Mannequin's materials are double-sided in the file, so they are drawn double-sided now. Of the 16 pictures drawn off screen, only 1 pixel of one changes. It was taken as looking the same.

**Made**
- `GltfModel`: an argument of the file's place to `load()`, `loadFile()`, `turnAbout()`, `uvs` (2 a vertex), `images`, `image_luminance`, `humanoid_bones`, `vrm_version`. `image`, `alpha`, `alpha_cutoff`, `double_sided` of `SkinPrimitive`.
- `humanoid`: `humanBoneOf()`, `HumanBoneNodes`, `findHumanBones()` (presets, maps, found, the reasons it cannot), `HumanoidRig::build()` (from the nodes' indices), `restTurn()`, `facingAngle()`. The presets `mixamo`, `vroid`, `vrm`.
- `figure_pack`: `FigureBodySpec::bones`, `skin`, `tint`, `FigureMotionSpec::bones`. A map object for `skeleton` (an unknown role, a value not a bone's name, an empty map are reasons a pack cannot be read). `skin` and `tint` for `paint`.
- `Figures3D`: reads a model with `loadFile()`, finds the bones with `findHumanBones()`, and turns it to the front. The poses that hang the legs and the arms are made in the corrected directions.
- `FigureUniform::Paint::Skin`, `Tint`.
- `SkinnedMesh`: textures (unit 3), the buffer of the texture coordinates, each part's transparency and double-sidedness, `setFigures()` (sent once for the shadow map and the scene) and the variant's argument of `draw()`, `hasTextured()`. An argument of definitions to `ShaderProgram::load()`. `Scene3D` has 4 shaders of the figures (of the scene and of the shadow map, each with its variant). `GLFrame::end()` unbinds unit 3 too.
- `test/test_gltf.h`: a tool of the tests that writes glTF (with specular-glossiness, KHR_texture_transform and required extensions too), and a humanoid of boxes at the Mannequin's bones of the roles (the presets' names, a prefix, an A pose, bent elbows, any facing, no normals, VRM 0.x and 1.0, a texture).

#### 2026-11-16: Moving a pack to the trash (`feature/3d-view`)

The two commits of 9-2 (1aa3a42, be711a2) were pushed. The user made a pack of a downloaded VRM model and checked that it is drawn on the screen.

**Decided (user)**
- A pack loaded stays at the next start (rcssmonitor copies no files of a pack and reads them where they are each time; what stays is the pack's name and the folder chosen in the settings, and the packs in the user's data folder, read by themselves). So that a pack can be removed within the application, an item that moves a pack's folder to the trash is added to the menu of the Packs button.

**Made**
- `FigurePacks::moveToTrash()`: moves a pack's folder, with all the files in it, to the trash (`QFile::moveToTrash()`, from Qt 5.15; it can be restored from there). It refuses a bundled pack, a folder without `figure.json`, the home folder, a root, and a user's data folder itself (where the packs are). If the trash does not take it, it refuses, deleting nothing. Once moved, the packs are looked for again the next time.
- The settings dialog: a submenu "Move a Pack to the Trash" at the end of the Packs button's menu (under a separator). It lists the user's packs (not the bundled ones; those that cannot be read too), with the folder where two have the same name; the tooltip is the folder. It is off when the user has no pack. Choosing one asks first (Cancel by default; telling the folder, and that it can be restored from the trash). If the pack moved was chosen in Model, the players become cylinders; if it was the folder chosen itself, the folder's setting is emptied. If it cannot be moved, the reason is told. It asks after the menu is done with the item (filling the list again deletes the item).
- `docs/FIGURE_PACKS.md`: the menu's item, and that no files of a pack are copied.

**Tests**
- Test `config_dialog`, added (with HOME and the XDG data folder in a temporary folder of the test's: run by hand too, it touches neither the user's packs nor the trash): the list of the user's packs (no bundled ones, the folder where the names are the same, one that cannot be read, the tooltip), the question (the name and the folder, Cancel by default, nothing done on Cancel), Yes moving the folder with its files to the trash (`Trash/files` and `Trash/info`), the players as cylinders as it was chosen, gone from the list, a pack that cannot be read and is not chosen, a pack that is the folder chosen itself (the folder's setting emptied), the item off once the last one is moved, the user's data folder staying.
- Test `figure_pack`, added (with HOME in a temporary folder): the folder of a pack moved and its files in the trash, the pack no longer found; a pack gone already, a bundled pack, a folder without `figure.json`, the home folder and a user's data folder (even with a `figure.json` in them) refused, nothing moved.
- Each test was checked to fail when what it checks was broken on purpose (16 ways: refusing a bundled pack, the folder being there, `figure.json` being there, refusing the home folder, refusing a user's data folder, looking for the packs again, back to the cylinders, emptying the folder's setting, not listing the bundled packs, the folder beside the same names, turning the item off, asking, Cancel by default, telling whether it was chosen, the items' folders, the mark of one that cannot be read). Refusing a root is not checked (a test cannot put a `figure.json` at a root).
- Clean builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`) (50 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) and autotools pass too.

#### 2026-11-16: Step 9-3, FBX (`feature/3d-view`)

The two commits of the trash (3ae3892, be385a5) were pushed.

**Decided**
- `ufbx.c` and `ufbx.h` of ufbx v0.23.1 (the tag of 2026-09-25; MIT or Unlicense) are bundled unchanged in `third_party/ufbx/` (the version and commit in its README). No warnings with `-W -Wall`. Built by CMake only (not in the build without 3D, nor in autotools).
- FBX is read by ufbx into the same data as glTF (`GltfModel`; `FbxModel::read()`). `GltfModel::load()` calls it, telling FBX by the file's head (`Kaydara FBX Binary`, `; FBX`) or its name (`.fbx`), so the humanoid layer, the figures and the drawing take it as they are.
  - Space: ufbx converts to y up and meters (`UFBX_SPACE_CONVERSION_MODIFY_GEOMETRY`: the meshes, the transforms and the animations changed), puts geometry transforms into the meshes, and makes the inheritance a plain one. ufbx's root node of the scene is not read.
  - Rest: the bind pose of the skin's clusters (`bind_to_world`), decomposed into the transform from the parent only where it differs from the node's transform (where the same, the node's transform as it is, keeping it to the last bit as the bundled ones).
  - Meshes: per node, per material part, split into triangles, the same corners made one vertex. Skinned ones with the clusters as joints (the inverse bind matrices `geometry_to_bone`), the four heaviest weights summing to 1. Those without a skin (and vertices without weights) fixed to the node. The texture coordinates turned from FBX's bottom up to from the image's top.
  - Materials: with a texture, the texture's colors only (exporters often write a default color with a texture); without, the base (diffuse) color times its factor. A texture embedded, or else looked for by the relative name the FBX writes, by the name as it is, and by its name alone beside the FBX and in `<FBX's name>.fbm/`. A texture with transparent pixels is a mask (0.5), double-sided. A material of an opacity from 0.01 to 0.99 is BLEND, double-sided (0 taken as not written).
  - Clips: each take baked by `ufbx_bake_anim()` into keys from 0 at 30 a second.
  - An FBX file without a skin (a file of clips of a skeleton only) sets `GltfModel::posed_rest` (its rest is not known: the pose it was saved in, the first frame from Blender's exporter).
- Clips from other files (Mixamo gives a file an action): the value of `motion.clips` is a clip's name, the name of a file of the pack (told by its extension), or an object of a `file` and a `clip` (`FigureClipSpec`).
  - A clip of another file is baked onto the motion's model's skeleton through the humanoid roles (`retargetClip()`: at 30 a second, the file's skeleton's pose read by `poseOf()` and put on the motion's by `apply()`; the bones without a role of the same names, the prefix aside, by their turns from rest). The file's bones by the motion's `skeleton`, else found from the file's names. A file is read once.
  - An FBX file whose rest is not known takes the rotations and places in the model of the bones of the same names (`boneKey()`: after the last `:`) of the motion's model (`restAs()`). At first only the rotations were taken, and the hips' rest place stayed the pose's, the hips' move off by a constant.
  - In place: the hips' horizontal drift across a clip (the end from the start) taken off in a line (`in_place` of `retargetClip()`). A clip of another file always; a clip of the motion's own model only if its hips go more than 0.05 hip heights (`ROOT_MOTION`) across it, so that the bundled clips, in place, are untouched.
  - The motion's model needs no mesh (`mesh_required` of `GltfModel::load()`). Then the gait analysis takes the height as twice the hips' height (the Mannequin: 1.829 m for 0.916 m; at first the height was 0, and the clips were taken as not walking). A clip without a name is the first of the model's own clips (not one added from another file).
- The guide for Mixamo (`docs/FIGURE_PACKS_MIXAMO.md`) and its template (`docs/figure_packs/mixamo/figure.json`): the character as FBX Binary in a T pose, the animations with the same character chosen as FBX Binary (Without Skin will do; In Place if offered). The template writes no `paint` (it draws as it is; the team colors in the guide). Mixamo's material is used by oneself, not given away.
- A document for the maintainers (`docs/FIGURE_ASSETS.md`) and a Blender script converting FBX to glTF (`tools/fbx_to_glb.py`). The importer's option to ignore leaf bones is off, as it lost the head, a leaf bone. A skeleton-only FBX file is used without converting it to glTF (that its rest is not known would stay in the glTF).
- The test files are made from the bundled Mannequin with Blender 5.3 (`test/data/fbx/make_fbx.py`): `figure.fbx` (the body: fewer triangles, a texture embedded, the role bones of Mixamo's names; as Blender writes: the root nodes scaled 100, the unit 1 cm), `walk.fbx` (the skeleton of the role bones only and the walk, the hips going a stride (1.28 m) a cycle; as Mixamo writes: the skeleton itself 100 times larger, the values in centimeters), `plane.fbx` and `plane_zup.fbx` (a square on a moved node, its texture `Holes.png` beside it; written z up too). About 450 kB together.

**Made**
- `third_party/ufbx/` (`ufbx.c`, `ufbx.h`, `LICENSE`, `README.md`).
- `src/view3d/fbx_model`: `FbxModel::isFbx()`, `read()`.
- `GltfModel`: `mesh_required` of `load()` and `loadFile()`, `clear()`, `addImage()` (shared by glTF and FBX), `posed_rest`. The height of a model without a mesh is 0.
- `humanoid`: `HumanoidRig::roleOf()`, `boneKey()`, `clipDrift()`, `retargetClip()`, `restAs()`.
- `figure_pack`: `FigureClipSpec` (`text()`), the four clips of `FigureMotionSpec`.
- `Figures3D`: the motion's clips read from the model or from other files (`ClipSource`, `readClips()`), put in place.
- `docs/FIGURE_PACKS.md` (FBX, the three ways of a clip, putting in place), `docs/FIGURE_PACKS_MIXAMO.md`, `docs/figure_packs/mixamo/figure.json`, `docs/FIGURE_ASSETS.md`, `tools/fbx_to_glb.py`.
- The tests' tool `test/test_gltf.h` got clips (`Animation`) and `fromModel()`, which writes a model read back as a file (each part on a node of its own, as one node takes one mesh).

**Tests**
- The bundled packs as before: compared with the build after 9-2, the materials, cuts, scalp, vertices, strides, each figure's looks, the joint matrices and the 16 pictures are all the same to the byte.
- Test `fbx_model` (new): telling FBX (the head and the name); `figure.fbx` (in meters, y up, its height within 1 cm of the glTF Mannequin's, 10 role bones within 1 mm of the Mannequin's, the vertices at rest within the Mannequin's bounds, the weights summing to 1, the bones found by Mixamo's names facing +z, the color of the embedded texture, white for the material of the texture, the color of the joints' material); `walk.fbx` (refused when a mesh is required, read without, `posed_rest`, 24 nodes, one clip's duration and keys from 0, the hips' height in meters, 24 bones by `restAs()`, a stride forward, the clip put on the body in place the glTF Mannequin's walk every 0.1 s with every role's dot above 0.9995 and the hips within 5 mm); `plane.fbx` (a square without a skin fixed to its node, where the node was moved, the texture coordinates' directions, the texture beside it, a mask double-sided, the texture's transparency, the same place written z up, found in `.fbm`, no texture if not found, not looked for beside when read from memory); a broken file (the reason, nothing left of the one read before).
- Test `humanoid`, added: `boneKey()`, `retargetClip()` (the number of keys and the last time; on another skeleton (an A pose, Mixamo's names) the role bones and the hips as put on directly; on the same skeleton, the fingers too), putting in place (`clipDrift()` of a clip whose root goes a stride; in place it is 0 and the hips are the original walk's; kept when not in place), `restAs()` (a skeleton in a pose under names of another prefix in the same places and rotations as the one at rest, its own bone kept).
- Test `figure_pack`, added: 7 errors of writing a clip (a number, empty, a missing file, an extension in capitals, an object without `file`, a missing file of an object, a name not a string), the three ways of reading a clip and `text()`, an FBX pack (the body and the motion's model `figure.fbx`, the walk `walk.fbx`, the others the glTF Mannequin (its bones found by their own names, not by the motion's `skeleton` `mixamo`): the speeds and cycles within 2% of the Mannequin's, the walking figure's legs and arm with dots above 0.995 with the Mannequin's), the guide's template drawn with files standing for Mixamo's, a walk of the motion's own model going forward put in place at the Mannequin's speed, the reasons (a file without clips, a missing clip's name with the clips listed, a file not of FBX, a clip not of walking).
- Test `figure_pack` got a pack whose motion's model is a glTF skeleton without a mesh (the body another file): the speeds within 2% of the Mannequin's.
- Mistakes found while making it: a clip without a name took a clip added from another file (now limited to the model's own clips). With a motion's model without a mesh, the height was 0 and the gait could not be analyzed (now estimated from the hips). The tests' tool put two parts' meshes on one node when writing back, losing one. It made the animations' accessors after the document had copied their list.
- Each test was checked to fail when what it checks was broken on purpose (35 of 37 ways: telling FBX (2 ways), the unit, the axes, the space conversion, decomposing the rest from the bind pose, `posed_rest`, the texture coordinates' direction, the weights summing to 1, the mask, `.fbm`, white for a material of a texture, the order of the rotation's components, the nodes' transforms, not reading the root node, clearing before reading, the extensions of clip files, checking an object's `file`, taking the rest, finding the skeleton again, putting the motion's own clip in place, putting another file's clip in place, the motion model's mesh (FBX and glTF), the last `:` of `boneKey()`, the fingers of the same names, the place and rotation of `restAs()`, the time of a clip's end, taking the hips' drift off, the fingers' turns, the height of a model without a mesh, the height from the hips (2 ways)). The 2 that passed: skipping weights of zero or of no cluster (none in the test files), and which of the base's and the diffuse's textures is looked at first (the same texture from Blender's exporter). The limit of a clip looked up by name was a needless line and went. At first 8 ways passed (the axes (a file z up was added), finding the skeleton again (the motion got a `skeleton`), a motion's model without a mesh (the pack above), and so on), so tests were added.
- Measured (Release, the work machine): reading `figure.fbx` (about 800 vertices, 65 joints) 1.1 ms, `walk.fbx` 0.9 ms, `plane.fbx` 0.1 ms. Reading a pack of the guide's template's shape (the body and 4 FBX clips) and analyzing its gaits, 10 ms (the bundled motion first, 6.5 ms). Drawing weighs the same as a glTF of as many vertices (the same data is drawn).
- Clean builds and tests pass with Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`) (51 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) and autotools pass too.

**The user's check, and a mistake found (9-3)**
- The user made a pack of a character (Y Bot) and animations downloaded from Mixamo, and saw it drawn.

The cause: the materials' names of the guide's example (Shirt, Pants) were not those of a real Mixamo character (the names differ from character to character). Reading and telling the reason worked right.

It tells that a character whose skin and clothes are one material with one texture can be colored only all over (`tint` colors the skin too, `uniform` draws a uniform in place of the clothes and the face). The table of reasons got a row for a material not found.

#### 2026-11-16: Consulting on step 9-4 (`feature/3d-view`)

The 3 commits of 9-3 (f7b0a9f to 84046cf) were pushed, and CI passed.

**Looked into**
- 100STYLE (the authors' page and Zenodo record 8127870): CC BY 4.0. The credit asked for is, for example, "The 100STYLE Dataset - Ian Mason". Captured with XSens at 60 fps. 23 joints (Hips, Chest to Chest4, Neck, Head, the left and right Collar, Shoulder, Elbow, Wrist, Hip, Knee, Ankle and Toe). The files are `<Style>_<Movement>.bvh`, the movements FW, BW, SW (walking forward, backward, sideways), FR, BR, SR (running), ID (idling) and TR (transitions). Each file has T-poses before and after; `Frame_Cuts.csv` has the ranges to cut. The zip of the BVH files (`100STYLE.zip`) is 1.5 GB.
- VRMA (the VRMC_vrm_animation 1.0 specification): glTF, with the roles mapped to nodes in `extensions.VRMC_vrm_animation.humanoid.humanBones.<role>.node`. The rest of the role nodes must be the "VRM T-pose". Only the hips translate, and nothing scales. The first animation is read. Expressions and the look-at are not read.
- BVH has no unit, but retargeting takes the hips' height as the unit, so the unit hardly matters.

**Decided (the user)**
- 9-4 is split in two, and walking and running mixed by the way the figure goes moved from 9-5 (bundled clips cannot be checked on screen with nothing using them). 9-4a reading BVH and VRMA. 9-4b walking and running backward and sideways made from 100STYLE and bundled, mixed by the way the figure goes.
- Bundled from 100STYLE: BW, SW, BR and SR of the Neutral style (or another plain one if there is none). If the sideways ones go one way only, the other way is made by mirroring. Walking, jogging and sprinting forward and idling stay the Mannequin's (UAL). If the seams look unnatural, FW and FR from 100STYLE are taken instead. The clips are loops of 1 or 2 cycles thinned to 30 fps, bundled as BVH, with a script (Python's standard library only) that makes them from the original zip. The credits of the Mannequin and the Human packs get 100STYLE and what was done to it (cut, thinned, looped).
- Claude downloads the 100STYLE zip and puts it outside the repository (`../100style/`).
- The optional clips' names in figure.json: `walk_back`, `jog_back`, `walk_left`, `walk_right`, `jog_left`, `jog_right`. With one side only, the other is made by mirroring. With neither backward nor sideways ones, as now (forward played backward, the hips turned).
- A preset `xsens` of 100STYLE's (XSens's) bone names is added. The names of CMU's and other BVH files mostly fit the present `mixamo` preset.
- The facing of BVH (up and forward) is found from the rest pose by itself, not written in `figure.json` (as the facing correction of 9-2).

#### 2026-11-16: Step 9-4a, BVH and VRMA (`feature/3d-view`)

**Decided**
- BVH is read by our own code (`BvhModel`: the format is simple, and no dependency is added). The ROOT and JOINTs become bones, an End Site a bone `<joint>_End`. The rest at the OFFSETs, not turned. The rotation channels are composed in the order written (`Zrotation Xrotation Yrotation`: R = Rz Rx Ry, as Blender reads them). A position channel places the bone in its parent in place of its OFFSET (as Blender). The unit is kept. A file cut short in its motion is read up to its whole frames.
- VRMA is read as glTF, the roles of `VRMC_vrm_animation` read in the same form as VRM 1.0's (`vrm_version` stays -1; the skeleton preset `vrm` uses them).
- `.vrma` and `.bvh` were added to the extensions of clip files. Either can be the motion's model (no mesh).
- Standing a skeleton up: once its roles are found (`rigModel()`), the axis nearest to the way from the hips to the head is taken as up, and if not +y, the model gets a quarter or a half turn; then it is turned to face +z as before. An upright model has exactly +y as that axis and does not change (even with its head a little off above its hips).
- A preset of bone names `xsens` (the names of 100STYLE's BVH): spine Chest, chest Chest2, upperChest Chest4 (the bone the shoulders hang from, as in VRM); Chest3 has no role. The presets are tried in the order quaternius, mixamo, vroid, xsens (Mixamo's `RightShoulder` is a shoulder, XSens's an upper arm; a skeleton of Mixamo's names still fits mixamo).
- Settling a motion capture (`settleCapture()`; see "Found" below): a model read from BVH is marked (`GltfModel::capture`), and after it is stood up and turned to face +z, its rest is stood on the ground above the origin (the body's lowest bone at y = 0), and a clip is moved and turned about the vertical so that the hips start above the origin, it faces +z on the whole (the mean way the hips' sides face) and comes down to the ground (the body's lowest bone across the clip). The body is the hips and the bones below them. What is moved is the highest of the hips and the bones above them that the clip moves (the hips, or a root above them). If that bone has no rotation keys, keys for the turn are made. Settling twice changes nothing.
- The tests' BVH and VRMA files are written by the tests from the Mannequin (`test/test_bvh.h`, `TestGltf::animationOf()`). The BVH has the role bones not turned at rest (as motion capture), with the unit, the axes (z up, six ways), the facing, the rotation order, position channels on every joint, the placing in a capture room (the root's OFFSET at the origin, the place and heading in the room), and a root above the hips (a Reference holding the placing; without rotation channels too) chosen. This form was taken at first because Blender was no longer on the work machine. After the user installed Blender again, the Mannequin's walk written by Blender's exporter (`test/data/bvh/walk.bvh`, `make_bvh.py`) was added too (an independent writer checking the reader).

**Found**
- Reading the real 100STYLE (the 8 files of Neutral) found the skeleton (xsens), up (+y), facing (+z) and the T pose (no arm correction) right, but the hips' height 0. 100STYLE's root OFFSET is at the origin (the legs below it), and its position channels are the place in the room it was taken in (the hips about 96 to 98 cm up, x and z moving meters). Drawn as it was, a figure would float as high as its hips and be off sideways by its place in the room. The tests' BVH had the root at the real height of the hips, so the tests missed it. The settling was added, and the tests' BVH got the placing in a capture room.
- The real files also walk every way in the room. The clips of walking and running must face +z, so the settling turns the mean heading of the hips to +z.
- Blender's BVH exporter puts a `root` (with channels) above the hips. The first settling did nothing when a bone above the hips moved, so it was widened to move the highest bone that the clip moves, and the ground is measured on the body (the hips and below) only (not misled by a root above the hips lying below the ground). Blender's exporter with positions on the root only (`root_transform_only`) also leaves out the hips' bobbing; the test file has positions on every joint.

**Made**
- `src/view3d/bvh_model`: `BvhModel::isBvh()` (`HIERARCHY` at the head, or `.bvh` in the name), `read()`.
- `GltfModel::load()`: BVH read by `BvhModel::read()` ("no mesh (a BVH file has a motion only)" when a mesh is required). VRMA's roles. `GltfModel::capture`.
- `humanoid`: `HumanoidRig::upAxis()`, `settleCapture()`, the preset `xsens`.
- `Figures3D` (`rigModel()`): standing up, settling.
- `figure_pack`: the extensions of clip files.

#### 2026-11-16: Step 9-4b, walking and running backward and sideways (`feature/3d-view`)

Pushed the two commits of 9-4a (e63ccfb, 385216f); the CI passed. 9-4b went on as the user told (as recommended).

**Looked into**
- In BW, SW, BR and SR of 100STYLE's Neutral, the cycles from the left foot coming down to it coming down again were found by the feet and the hips (the room is small and the actor turns all the time, so cycles going straight were chosen). The cycles chosen are in the table of `docs/FIGURE_ASSETS.md` (walking backward 6514 to 6585, running backward 2755 to 2795, walking to the right 5064 to 5133, running to the left 547 to 591; going 174 to 179° and 88° from the body direction). Walking to the left and running to the right had no straight cycles, so the other sides are mirrored (which checks telling one side only, too).
- The speeds (for 1.85 m): walking backward 0.54, running backward 1.00, walking sideways 0.42, running sideways 1.36 m/s. The players of the log (`1-json.rcg.gz`) go backward at a median of 2.16 m/s (90% 4.90) and sideways at 1.28 m/s (90% 3.26): much faster than the clips.
- 100STYLE's running backward and walking sideways shuffle with the feet low (the lower of the ankle and the toes lifts only 3 to 5 cm), short of a walk's "feet lifted" (2% of the height).
- Players of the log running fast go a little off their body direction (at 3 to 5 m/s: under 2° 34%, 2 to 10° 46%, 10 to 30° 10%; at 5 m/s and faster, 2 to 10° 48%). With weights by the strides, the forward stride (3 to 5 m) being longer than the sideways one (1 m), going only 5 to 10° off mixes in 20 to 35% of the sideways clips.

**Decided**
- Fast: backward, faster than the backward clips, mixed with the forward clips played backward (2 m/s backward: 80% the running backward, 20% the forward jog played backward, 1.2 cycles a second). Sideways, faster than the running sideways, its stride kept and the steps quicker (3 m/s straight sideways: 3 cycles a second; about a tenth of the log's sideways moves).
- Up to 30° from forward and backward taken by the hips' turn (for the last finding above). Nine tenths of the players running fast are drawn with the forward clips and the hips turned; the farther off to the side, the more the sideways clips mix in. Straight sideways, the hips do not turn.
- A clip played backward takes the time its left foot leaves the ground as its phase 0 (played backward, the foot comes down there). All clips are mixed with the left foot coming down at the same time.
- A foot counts as lifted from 1% of the height (the idle clip is still read as no walk).
- Mirroring is of the humanoid pose (each role's rotation swapped left and right, the rotation's axis mirrored across x = 0, the hips' move with x the other way). The bones without a role (the fingers) are not mirrored (as they are).

#### 2026-11-16: Step 9-5a, the clips of the actions (`feature/3d-view`)

**Looked into**
- The four clips of UAL2 were drawn offscreen. `Slide_Start` goes from running feet first onto the ground, sliding nearly on the back (the hips 5 cm high; with root motion, 4.5 m in 0.83 s). `Slide_Exit` springs up from it and runs off (0.5 s). `Hit_Knockback` doubles up, then flies back, the feet a meter up, and lands on the back (with root motion, 3 m back). `LayToIdle` gets up from lying on the back (1.53 s).
- In the logs, a player moves soon after its bit ends (the full game logs: after a tackle, a median of 0.38 m in 2 cycles, 1.68 m in 5; after being fouled, 0.44 m in 2 cycles, 1.85 m in 5). Getting up must end 2 to 3 cycles after the bit (the present `TACKLE_TAIL` and `FOULED_TAIL`).

**Decided**
- `Hit_Knockback` is not bundled (within what the user agreed: the poses made here if a clip does not fit): it flies back with the feet higher than a meter, unlike a player tripped by a foul. A fouled player falls in the pose made here (sitting down, leaning back on the hands) and gets up by `LayToIdle`. So a clip of getting up plays from the pose down whether a clip's or made here.
- Bundled: `Slide_Start` (`tackle`), `Slide_Exit` (`tackle_up`), `LayToIdle` (`get_up`). `Slide_Loop` is not used (the slide goes on in the last pose of `Slide_Start`).
- Added to the names consulted on: `kick_tap` (a touch; without it the pose made here, not `kick`, so that dribbling does not swing a full kick at every touch), `tackle_up` (getting up from the slide; without it `get_up`), and `dive_left` (with one side told, the other mirrored, as for walking sideways). `turn_right` is 9-5b's.
- The actions' timing (their weights rising and falling) is that of the poses made here; within it a clip plays at its own speed, its moment at the moment of the log. A kick swings back from a cycle before its moment, as the pose made here does (the log is looked at only two frames ahead).
- The moment (found): a kick, when a foot (its toes, or else the ankle) swings forward fastest (that foot kicks; the other plays the clip mirrored); slower than twice the hips' height a second, the clip does not kick. The slide and a fall, when the hips come down to 40% of their height (on the ground). The catch and the dive, when the middle of the hands is farthest from the chest. A fall plays from `FOULED_START` (its moment, when it is on the ground, is for getting up).
- Getting up (`upSchedule()`): to end with the action (a tackle at last + `TACKLE_TAIL`, a foul at last + `FOULED_TAIL`, a dive at hold + `CATCH_TAIL`), after the body is down, as late as it can at its own speed; faster if the time is short (up to twice); its start left out if that is not enough (`LayToIdle` from sitting up). It takes over from the pose down in a cycle.
- The catch: `catch_low` and `catch_high` mixed by the ball's height (with one of them, the other height in the pose made here); the body turned toward the ball by up to 50°. The dive: mixed in for a ball farther to the side than 0.8 m, all of it from 1.2 m (`diveOf()`); with a clip of the dive, the lean (`rollOf()`) less as much.
- A clip going somewhere (its hips farther across than 30% of their height: the slide, the dive) has its hips kept horizontally where they start (`ClipPlace::Pinned`). The log decides where the player is.
- A pack without clips of the actions poses them as before (without a clip of getting up, the poses made here as they were).

**Made**
- `FigureClipSpec::moment`, the 12 optional clips of `FigureMotionSpec` (`actionKeys()`, `actionClips()`). Why not (a `moment` not a number of 0 or more).
- `clipTravel()`, `ClipPlace`, `retargetClip()` taking a `ClipPlace` (`humanoid`).
- `ActionClip`, `ActionClips`, `Figures3D::actionClips()`, `kickMoment()`, `downTime()`, `reachMoment()`, `upSchedule()`, `diveOf()`, `rise()`, `catchPose()` (the catch made here moved into a function). Why not ("is not of kicking (no foot swings forward)", "is not of falling (the hips do not come down)"; read with a `moment` told).
- Bundled: `Actions.glb` (120 kB) and `pack_ual2_actions.py`, which writes it (Python's standard library only; the skeleton and three clips, the channels keeping their nodes at rest left out). The Mannequin pack's `figure.json` (the three clips and the credit), both packs' READMEs.
- Test tools: `TestGltf::poseAnimation()` (a clip of poses) and `skeletonFile()` (a file of a skeleton and clips only).
- Documents: `docs/FIGURE_PACKS.md` ("The actions"), `docs/FIGURE_PACKS_MIXAMO.md` (the animations of the actions), `docs/FIGURE_ASSETS.md` (how the clips of the actions are made).

**Tests**
- `figures_3d`: the tests of the poses made here (the tackle, the foul, the human body sitting) now use a pack without clips of the actions (`forwardOnly()` takes them off too). Added: `upSchedule()` (late at its own speed, faster, twice with its start left out, no time; each ending with the action), the bundled actions (which clips there are and their moments; a tackle held 10 cycles on the ground from its moment, still sliding before getting up, standing near its end; a tackle of one cycle getting up at once, faster; a fouled player standing while the tackler slides in, sitting as the pose made here, getting up).
- `figure_pack`: the bundled pack's clips of the actions, reading them (the 12 keys, `moment`, -1 if not told, unequal by the moment), five rows of why not. `actionPacks()` (new): with clips made of poses on the Mannequin's skeleton (a kick with the left foot, a slide going forward, getting up, a catch, a dive to the right): the kick (the left foot's clip and the right foot's mirrored, its moment at 0.5 s, the pass and the shot by `kick`, no touch, a moment told), the slide (its moment, pinned not going anywhere, getting up by `get_up`), the catch and the dive (their moments, the left mirrored), the poses (the kicking foot before the moment as the clip's, both feet, the tackle after its moment as the clip's, halfway up as the clip of getting up, between the two while taking over, the catch as its clip and turned 40° toward the ball, the dive's weight and no lean, the head's side to the right and to the left), a motion differing only in a moment read again, a dive not going down, a clip of the motion's own model pinned too, two reasons of why not and read with a `moment` told.

#### 2026-11-16: Step 9-5b, turning on the spot (`feature/3d-view`)

Pushed the two commits of 9-5a (d95c985, 20e007e); the CI passed (checked by the user). The work's scratch folder moved and the builds before were gone, so the Qt6, Qt5, no-3D and autotools builds were made again.

**Looked into (the full game log `1-json.rcg.gz`)**
- A standing player (slower than 0.6 m/s) turns its body about 30 times a minute. Most turns take one cycle (the median 1, 90% within 3 cycles), 57° at the median, 90% up to 180°. A standing player turns 84° in a cycle at the median.
- At that speed, stepping only with the body would take a step in 0.1 s, too busy.

**Decided**
- The legs turn behind the body direction: the upper body turns with the log at once, the legs follow the body direction averaged over the last 3 cycles (from the frames before only, so it fits both the rule of §5.2 and the live delay of two cycles). The lag is taken by the hips' turn (up to 45°), the chest facing the body direction.
- Standing, the legs step by how far they turn: the walk at half weight, a cycle (two steps) for each 90° they turn; turning 90°, about two steps in 0.3 s. The weight is by how fast they turn (all at 180°/s) and how much the figure stands (what the gait's mix leaves).
- The clips of turning (`turn_left`, `turn_right`): added to the names consulted on (`turn_right`, the left mirrored) is `turn_left`, one side told giving the other mirrored (as the dive). A clip is played at the time of how far the legs have turned, its own turn taken off (the log decides the facing). None is bundled.

**Made**
- `GaitTable`: the body direction unwrapped, its sum, the legs' direction, how far they have turned, the frame from which they follow (`Entry`), `headingAt()`, `areaAt()`, `legsAt()`, `TURN_LAG`, `TURN_SPAN`. The number of the frames dropped live (`M_dropped`). `turned`, `lag`, `turn_rate` of `FigureMotion`.
- `Figures3D`: `turnAngle()`, `turnStepOf()`, the stepping and the hips' turn in `localPose()`, `TURN_TWIST_MAX`, `TURN_PER_CYCLE`, `TURN_RATE_FULL`, `TURN_STEP`, `TURN_MIN`. `turn`, `turn_angle`, `turn_times` of `ActionClips`.
- `turn_left`, `turn_right` of `FigureMotionSpec` (14 optional clips now). Why not ("is not of turning (the hips do not turn)", "is not of turning to the right (it turns to the left)").
- Documents: `docs/FIGURE_PACKS.md` ("Turning on the spot" and a row of the table), `docs/FIGURE_PACKS_MIXAMO.md` (the animations of turning and a row of why not).

**Tests**
- Added to `gait_table`: the legs' direction (halfway through a 90° turn a lag of 41.25°; a cycle after it, 45°, a turning speed of 300°/s and 45° turned; in a line between the frames; 3 cycles after, caught up, 90° turned; nothing before), to the left (the lag and the speed the other way, as far turned), across ±180° (the short way, 20°), after a move and a player out of the game (starting again, not counted as turned), live (frames added one by one the same, the oldest dropped the same, dropped after a move the same).
- Added to `figures_3d` (`turning()`): the hips turned toward the legs by the lag (up to 45°), the chest facing the body direction, not when running, the stepping's weight (by how fast they turn, standing), the same pose as before without turning, steps 0.3 to 0.7 times the walk's, half a cycle at 45° (the feet the other way), a cycle at 90°.

The bundled pack's 14 keys, a row of why not.
- Each test was checked to fail with what it checks broken for a while (29 ways: the angle not unwrapped, the sum not by trapezoids, not starting again after a move, nor out of the game, the jump of starting again counted, the window's length, starting again ignored, the sum between the frames, the speed's sign, the lag's sign, the frames dropped not counted, the turn not in a line between the frames, the hips' turn's sign, the chest not turned back, the lag not held at 45°, the lag when running, standing ignored, stepping always all, the walk's steps at full size, the stepping's angle a cycle, the clip's own turn not taken off, the clip's side ignored, the clip not played again, the angle's sign, the times in proportion to the angle, the check of turning, the check of the way, the left not mirrored, the left's angle). At first four ways (the frames dropped not counted, the turn not in a line between the frames, the stepping's angle a cycle, the times in proportion to the angle) were missed; tests were added (dropping after a move, the turn between the frames, the angle by a number, not the constant, the time at a quarter of an eased clip).
- Measured (Release, the work machine): with all 22 figures standing and turning, the poses take 0.35 to 0.38 ms a frame (0.25 ms just standing). The tables of the full game logs take 5.9 ms for 6,209 frames and 7.8 ms for 8,406 (4.5 ms and 5.7 ms before). The standing players step (their legs turning faster than 90°/s) about 30% of the time, lagging 76 to 82° then on average (the hips' turn held at 45°).
- Drawn offscreen through their times: turns of 90° and 170° (a synthetic log), and a real moment of the full game log (a player slowing down to stand, turning, and running off).
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the build and the tests pass (52 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) and autotools pass too.

**The user's remark and the change (9-5b)**
- The user's remark: the stepping is far too busy. However fast, it should be less than a step a cycle.
- The cause: a step each 45° the legs turned, with no limit. A turn of 90° made about 0.67 steps a cycle (6.7 a second), one of 170° about 1.3. In two full game logs, while stepping, the steps were 12 to 13 a second on the weighted average (6 to 7 at the median, 20 at 90%, 36 to 40 at most).
- Decided (the user judges it on screen): a step each 90° the legs turn (45° before), at most 0.25 steps a cycle (2.5 a second: a person stepping quickly steps 2 to 3 times a second). Turning faster, the legs turn the rest without a step (catching up with the body direction as before). A turn of 90° makes about 0.8 steps in 0.4 s; one of 170°, a step.
- Fixed: `GaitTable` keeps the steps taken (`FigureMotion::steps`, in place of `turned`), adding at each frame the legs' turn / `TURN_STEP_ANGLE` (90°), held to `TURN_STEPS_MAX` (0.25). From the frames before only, so the same paused, played back, and live. `Figures3D` plays the walk a cycle each two steps (`TURN_PER_CYCLE` is gone), and a clip of turning at the time of its angle of the steps times 90°.
- In the two full game logs: while stepping, 2.2 to 2.3 steps a second on the weighted average, 2.5 at most.
- Tests changed: `gait_table` (a turn of 90°: a sixth of a step, the limit of a quarter twice, a sixth; in a line between the frames; a turn of 170°: at most a quarter in each cycle, a step in all; 20°: 2/9 of a step), `figures_3d` (a step on, the other foot; two steps, back), `figure_pack` (a clip of turning played at its angle of the steps times 90°). Each was checked to fail with what it checks broken (7 ways: no limit, a limit of 0.5, a step each 45°, counted at starting again, not in a line between the frames, the walk's cycle a step, the clip's step 45°).
- Drawn offscreen through their times: a player of the full game log standing and turning (the foot moving slowly part of a step).
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the build and the tests pass (52 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) passes too (autotools does not build the 3D files, so it does not change).

**What remains**
- A clip of turning also plays at most 67.5° of it a cycle (675° a second; after the change below). By the steps in the clip, it may look busy (to be seen when checking with Mixamo's clips).

**The user's remark and the change, 2 (9-5b)**
- The user's remark: at a free kick and so on, turning fast (nearly 180°), a figure does not step to be seen. That is the same as before anything was made. Turning fast, it should step at a speed to be seen, even if no person can.
- The cause (a turn of 147° at a kick-in in the full game log): the hips' turn held at 45°, the legs on screen turn about 100° in a cycle. The steps were held to a quarter of a step a cycle, a step in 0.4 s, at half the walk's size: the feet only turned on the ground.
- Decided (the user judges it on screen): a step each 90° as it was, the limit raised to 0.75 steps a cycle (7.5 a second: fewer than one a cycle, as the user said first). The legs turn at most 60° a cycle (a third of a turn of 180°), so a single turn does not reach the limit and the steps go with the angle (a step for 90°, about 1.6 for 147°, 2 for 180°); only turns in a row reach it. A turn of up to about 67° in 0.3 s steps as with the change before (the limit of a quarter). A step each 60° (about 3 steps, as a person turning 180° on the spot) was not taken: it steps more in ordinary turns too, nearer the first busy look.
- Fixed: `GaitTable::TURN_STEPS_MAX` is 0.75.
- In the two full game logs: while stepping, 5.0 to 5.3 steps a second on the weighted average (3.1 to 3.5 at the median, 7.5 at 90% and at most). The turn of 147° at the kick-in makes 1.6 steps in 0.4 s.
- Tests changed: `gait_table` (a turn of 90° not held: a sixth, a third, a third and a sixth of a step, a step in all; a turn of 170°: 170/90 steps; spinning 170° a cycle twice: the three cycles in the middle held, every cycle at most the limit). Each was checked to fail with what it checks broken (6 ways: no limit, a limit of 0.25, a limit of 1, a step each 45°, counted at starting again, not in a line between the frames).
- Drawn offscreen through its times: the turn of 147° at the kick-in (the feet up in turn).
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the build and the tests pass (52 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) passes too.
- The user's check: played at half speed, the motion looks fairly good (the poses come from the frame and the fraction only, so the interpolation does not change; the server's players turn nearly 180° in a cycle, faster than people). This is committed as a compromise for now, and a fast turn as a pivot (turning on one foot, the other leg swung once widely) is to be tried (user).
- Tried (each compared by the user side by side with the version committed, which the user found better, so they were discarded): (1) a quick turn as a pivot (the legs 30° to 75° behind, the leg on the side turned to lifted and turned with the hips, in place of the steps): it spun on one foot like a skater, and looked stranger. (2) Turning the head, the chest and then the hips (with the hips' turn held at 45°, in a turn of nearly 180° the legs on screen turn about 100° in the first 0.1 s; the hips following the legs' direction by up to 135°, the chest up to 45° ahead of them, the head at the body direction), with fewer steps: a step each 120°, at most 0.5 steps a cycle.

#### 2026-11-16: The goalie's dive and tackle (a consultation)

The record of the trials of 9-5b (85e36a9) was pushed.

**Looked into**
- The user's question: is the goalie's dive made?
- The bundled packs have no clip of a dive. By the pose made here, the hands go out to the ball at a catch, and with the ball more than 0.5 m to the side of the body the figure leans that way (35° at most, from 2 m). A pack with `dive_right` or `dive_left` mixes in the clip of a dive with the ball from 0.8 m to 1.2 m to the side (9-5a).
- In four full game logs, the goalies catch 0 to 3 times a game (7 in all). The ball is at most 0.80 m to the side; no catch is beyond 0.8 m. Even with a clip of a dive, the present values would not show it (the lean is about 7° at most).
- The CC0 material that can be bundled has no jump forward and no punching. UAL1 Standard has jumps straight up on the spot (Jump_Start, Jump_Loop, Jump_Land), a roll forward (Roll) and boxing punches (Punch_Jab, Punch_Cross); UAL2 Standard has NinjaJump, Shield_Dash, Sword_Dash and so on.
- The goalies tackle twice in all in the four full game logs (the others 20 to 42 times a game). It is drawn as the others' slide.

**Decided (user)**
- In the simulation a tackle works much as a dive does, so the dive stays as it is (the lean only).

**What remains (a task for later, user)**
- The goalie's tackle as a jump forward punching the ball away. By a pose made here (jumping forward, both fists thrust forward and up, landing and getting up), or by an optional clip of a pack (e.g. `goalie_tackle`).

### Phase 9 (continued)

#### 2026-11-16: Step 9-6, finishing (measuring) (`feature/3d-view`)

**Decided (user)**
- 9-6 goes in the order of measuring, looking over the documents, and the user's check. The user's Mixamo pack (`test-mixamo`: the Y Bot body and four Mixamo motions) may be read, only, for measuring (no Mixamo material in the results).

**How it was measured**
- The real `FieldView3D` in a window not shown (logical 1920×1080; 2480×1395 physical at the screen's scale of 1.29), playing a full game log at the normal speed from frame 3,000, drawn every 16 ms (`RCSSMONITOR_3D_TIMING`). 22 figures, the broadcast camera, realistic grass, the sky, the stands, the boards and the banners, the ball's pattern, ring, trail and flight. Simple shadows (the default) and detailed ones.
- The heavy packs: the bundled male body subdivided in Blender (once: 105k triangles; twice: 420k), each of its six materials with a texture of 2048 px (in the work's temporary place; not in the repository).
- Noticed: this integrated GPU seems to lower its clock under a light load, so the same conditions give other times on other days (7.7 ms today for the 5.6 ms of 8-5). The commit of 9-2 (be711a2) built and measured in turn with the present build gives the same (3.9 to 4.2 ms), so the program did not get slower. Times are compared only between those measured together.

**Results (Release, the work machine's integrated GPU (radeonsi), the GPU's time of a frame)**

| Pack | A body's vertices / triangles | Loading | Simple shadows | Detailed shadows |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mannequin | 9k / 14k | (bundled; at start) | 4.0 ms | 7.1 ms |
| Human (men and women) | 10k to 16k / 17k to 22k | 40 ms | 4.6 ms | 7.7 ms |
| test-mixamo (Y Bot) | 35k / 55k | 44 to 60 ms | 9.6 ms | 17.2 ms (52 fps) |
| Heavy 1 | 53k / 105k | 300 to 400 ms | 18.9 ms (48 fps) | 30.8 ms (30 fps) |
| Heavy 2 | 211k / 420k | 350 to 440 ms | 57 ms (15 fps) | 104 ms (9 fps) |

- The CPU's building takes 0.7 to 1.0 ms a frame with any pack.
- The cause looked into: with a window of a sixteenth of the pixels, the figures' time hardly lessens (5.6 ms to 5 ms for Y Bot); without multisampling, the same; with less work in the vertex shader (no inverse matrix for the normals, no reading of joints of weight 0), about a tenth less. On this integrated GPU, handling many small triangles moved by bones is itself heavy, and the time goes roughly with the vertices and the triangles. Detailed shadows draw the figures once more, into the shadow map: nearly twice.
- The triangles' order (the vertices moved by bones for each triangle drawn; the GPU reuses the vertices just done): the Mannequin 0.82, Human 1.45, Y Bot 1.17, the heavy packs 0.75 to 0.85 (0.6 to 0.7 at best).

**Fixed**
- At loading, the triangles of each part of a mesh are ordered (`GltfModel::orderTriangles()`; Tipsify: Sander, Nehab and Barczak 2007; looking the same: the triangles and their winding kept, only their order changed). For glTF, VRM and FBX alike. Then: the Mannequin 0.72, Human 0.71 to 0.81, Y Bot 0.74, the heavy packs 0.65 to 0.67.
- The gain (measured together): Human, detailed shadows 7.7 to 6.8 ms, simple 4.6 to 4.2 ms; Y Bot, detailed 17.2 to 16.4 ms (55 fps), simple 9.6 to 9.1 ms; Heavy 1, simple 18.9 to 18.0 ms.
- Tests: `gltf_model` (`triangleOrder()`): the triangles of a 40×40 grid shuffled and turned round (over 2) come under 0.75, the same triangles with their winding; too few triangles, and a triangle of a vertex twice; each part of the Human male body read under 0.9, all of it under 0.72 (0.708). `fbx_model` (`figure()`): `figure.fbx` shares few vertices (1.9 a triangle), so within 0.15 of the vertices it uses (2.10 as read, 2.00 ordered). Each was checked to fail with what it checks broken (6 ways: not ordered for glTF, nor for FBX, no priority, the winding changed, no going back to the dead ends, the next fan not from the candidates). At first the FBX test's bar was absolute (under 0.9) and did not pass (the vertices not shared), and not going back to the dead ends could not be told; the bars were fixed.
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the build and the tests pass (52 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) passes too.

#### 2026-11-17: Step 9-6, the speed on screens of high resolution (`feature/3d-view`)

The two commits of the measuring (0720c49, a93aad9) were pushed.

**The user's remark**
- Maximized on a 4K screen (3840×2160, the screen's scale 1.29), the Mannequin with simple shadows makes about 36 fps, and even the cylinders do not reach 40. Is a screen of high resolution hopeless with a GPU in the CPU?

**Looked into**
- Measured in a window not shown at 3840×2160 physical, the GPU draws the 3D scene in 4.4 ms with the cylinders and 6.2 ms with the Mannequin; without multisampling, or with grass in stripes and no stands, not much less.
- Measured by the user on the real screen (`RCSSMONITOR_3D_TIMING`) at 3840×2069 physical: CPU 0.7 ms and GPU 6.2 to 6.5 ms, yet 32 fps (about 31 ms a frame). The rest went to Qt putting the frame drawn on the screen (a QOpenGLWidget draws into an image of its own, its multisampling resolved, and Qt puts it together with the main window's image on the screen: a few copies of the whole view in every frame).
- With a test program (a main window with a tool bar, drawing all the time a picture that takes the GPU about 5 ms at 4K; in the work's temporary place), the user measured, maximized on the 4K screen:

| Way | Multisampling 4x | None |
|---|---|---|
| QOpenGLWidget (as it was) | 30.7 fps | 41.5 fps |
| A QOpenGLWindow embedded | 48.3 fps | 55.7 fps |

**Decided (user)**
- The 3D view draws straight into a window (QOpenGLWindow). Smooth edges (multisampling) can be turned off by an option.

**Made**
- `FieldView3D` is a QOpenGLWindow, put in the main window by `QWidget::createWindowContainer()`. It takes no keyboard focus (`Qt::WindowDoesNotAcceptFocus`; nor does its container; the keys are all the main window's shortcuts). The focus goes to the 2D view or the stack of the views (QStackedWidget) (`MainWindow::focusView()`). The events of an embedded window do not go up to its parent, so the 3D view tells of the wheel (`FieldView3D::wheelTurned()`), and the main window steps the frames as for the 2D view (`MainWindow::stepByWheel()`). A QWindow has no context menu events, so the referee's menu shows on the right button's press.
- Smooth edges by the window's format would make the window again at every change, so they are drawn into a multisampled framebuffer of our own (`gl/render_target`, `RenderTarget`: renderbuffers of color, depth and stencil; at most the GPU's samples) and copied into the window's (without samples) (`Scene3D::render()`'s `samples`). Turned on and off as the frames go.
- An option `antialias_3d` (on by default, 4 samples; group `View3D`) and the command line's `--antialias-3d`. Smooth Edges in the Guides of the dialog's 3D tab; back by Default. The measuring line (`RCSSMONITOR_3D_TIMING`) says ` smooth` with smooth edges.

**Tests**
- `scene_3d` (`smoothEdges()`): a scene with detailed shadows too, drawn without samples and with 4: with 4, all of the framebuffer drawn over (magenta first, none left), other only at the edges (more than 100 pixels, less than a tenth of all), there between the colors around (the samples averaged), the head's middle the same, the same in every frame, and without samples again as before. `config_dialog`: Smooth Edges' tooltip, on at first, the option changed by a click, back by Default, Default's tooltip.
- Each was checked to fail with what it checks broken (6 ways: not copied, not smoothed, half copied, the wrong framebuffer after the shadow map, the dialog not changing the option, Default not setting it back). One sample could not be told on this driver (it seems to take more samples than asked). At first, half copied (the frame before was left in the framebuffer) and the wrong framebuffer after the shadow map (the scene had no detailed shadows) were missed; tests were added.
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the build and the tests pass (52 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) and autotools pass too.

**The user's check**
- The keys, the mouse (turning, moving, nearer, the wheel), the referee's menu, switching 2D and 3D, the dialogs and menus over the view, and the HUD's places are fine.
- Maximized on the 4K screen (3840×2069 physical): 42 to 54 fps with smooth edges, about 44 without (32 fps before). Making the window larger, from some size it suddenly cannot keep 60 fps. The GPU's time goes nearly with the pixels (about 1.3 to 1.5 ms a million pixels): 2.8 ms at 1345×889, 5.4 ms at 2415×1573, 9.3 ms at 3006×1912, 9.4 to 11.8 ms at 3840×2069. The desktop's (GNOME's) compositing uses the same GPU, and a frame over 16.7 ms in all waits for the next refresh (33 ms): beyond 5 to 6 million pixels it falls suddenly. At 4K, without smooth edges the GPU's time is hardly less (drawing the grass, the sky and so on over the whole screen is itself heavy). Without smooth edges in a small window, `submit` takes about 10 ms: waiting for the screen's refresh (60 fps).

#### 2026-11-17: Step 9-6, the CI's failure, XWayland and Wayland, the right click on Wayland (`feature/3d-view`)

The two commits of the QOpenGLWindow (393f02d, fd35a21) were pushed.

**The CI's failure**
- In every push after 9-5b, only `figure_pack` (`turnPacks()`) of the jobs CMake, Qt6 and CMake, Qt5 (Ubuntu 26.04) failed (the hips off by 2.25°, 2° allowed). The jobs of Ubuntu 22.04 and autotools passed.
- The cause: the table of "the time it has turned each degree" of a clip of turning (`Figures3D::turnAngle()`) was made of the times the clip was read at, every 1/60 s, the first past each degree. Not in a line between them, as late as one time apart: 2.25° where it turns fastest. Whether the times fall just right at 45° was split by small differences of calculation: 0° here, 2.25° on the CI.
- Fixed: in a line between the times read (less than 0.0001° at any degree). The test checks less than 0.1° at a few degrees from 10° to 80°, and two values of the table within 0.002 s. It was checked to fail without the line between. With commit 657bfba, the CI's four jobs passed.

**Drawing at a lower resolution (tried, not taken)**
- As a 4K screen does not reach 60 fps, an option `resolution_3d` was made to draw the 3D picture at Full, 3/4 or 1/2 and make it larger to the screen's size (drawn small into the `RenderTarget`, copied by `GL_LINEAR`; with smooth edges, resolved at the same size first, then made larger).
- Measured by the user on the 4K screen, with smooth edges: Full 40 fps (GPU 10.2 ms), 3/4 43 fps (11.3 ms), 1/2 47 fps (8.5 ms). The GPU's time is hardly less.
- Measured without a window, the same scene drawn into a framebuffer of 3840×2160 (a tool in the work's temporary place; with smooth edges / without):

| Resolution | With smooth edges | Without |
|---|---|---|
| Full | 9.95 ms | 5.52 ms |
| 3/4 | 8.08 ms | 5.41 ms |
| 1/2 | 5.47 ms | 4.19 ms |

- Found: Full without smooth edges takes about the time of 1/2 with them, and is sharp. Copying from a lower resolution larger to 4K takes about 1.5 ms (drawing into a framebuffer of nearly the whole size takes 1.4 to 1.7 ms more than drawing straight), and the shapes of the figures and the stands cost the same at any resolution (even with cylinders and no stands, 1.4 ms at 1/4). Without a window it draws 91 to 229 frames a second, so the window's 40 to 47 fps are set by the way to the screen more than by the drawing. That the GPU's time in the window is not less at a lower resolution is guessed to be the GPU's clock going down with less load (not confirmed).
- A correction: the last record's "at 4K, without smooth edges the GPU's time is hardly less" came from times measured in the window. Measured without a window, smooth edges take about 4.4 ms at 4K. The GPU's times measured in the window are not fit for comparing.

**XWayland and Wayland**
- On the work machine, Qt 6.4 picks XWayland (xcb) even in GNOME's Wayland session. Qt's plugin of Wayland (qt6-wayland) is installed.
- The test program (an embedded QOpenGLWindow drawing all the time a picture that takes the GPU about 5 ms at 4K), measured by the user maximized at 4K:

| Platform | Multisampling 4x | None |
|---|---|---|
| xcb (XWayland) | 43.7 fps | 50.2 fps |
| wayland | 59.8 fps | 59.8 fps |

- rcssmonitor with `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=wayland`: 59 to 60 fps at 4K (3840×2090) even with smooth edges (GPU 9.6 ms, as through XWayland). XWayland was the cause of the slowness at 4K.

**Decided (user)**
- No option to draw at a lower resolution (on Wayland, Full makes 60 fps; it does what turning smooth edges off does). The changes made were thrown away.
- Running on Wayland is told in the README (Phase 10): in a Wayland session with a screen of high resolution such as 4K, `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=wayland` makes the 3D view smooth. rcssmonitor does not pick Wayland by itself (Qt 6.4 picks XWayland on GNOME on purpose; the menus show off in full screen; newer Qt may decide by itself).

**The right click on Wayland**
- The user's remark: on Wayland, the right click's menu of the 3D view cannot be closed (that of the 2D view can). A right click on the 3D view also warned `requestActivate() called for FieldView3D ... which has Qt::WindowDoesNotAcceptFocus set.`
- The cause: on Wayland, while a menu (xdg_popup) is open, the clicks on the application's own surfaces go to them. Qt closes the menu on a click on a widget, but the 3D view is an embedded window, not a widget, so Qt 6.4 does not. Through X11 (XWayland too), the menu grabs the mouse, so it does not happen.
- Fixed: `PopupCloser` (`src/view3d/popup_closer`): an event filter on the 3D view's window. A press on the view (a double click too) while a menu (and its submenus) is open closes them, the click going no further (as through X11). While one is open, the wheel goes no further either (no frames stepped). The main window puts it on the 3D view when it makes it. It works for the menus of the menu bar and the tool bar too.
- Tests: `popup_closer` (new): with no menu, the press, the release, the double click and the wheel reach the window. With a menu open, the wheel does not reach it and the menu stays open; a right press closes the menu and does not reach the window; so does a double click; closed, they reach it again. A menu and its submenu both closed. A menu opened by `exec()` (as the referee's) ends by the click, with no item. Each was checked to fail with what it checks broken (5 ways: the click passed on, not closed, only one closed, the wheel passed on, the double click not seen). That the main window puts the filter on is not checked (the tests without a screen cannot make the 3D view).
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13, the build and the tests pass (53 tests). `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (19) and autotools pass too.
- Checked by the user on Wayland: a click on the 3D view closes the right click's menu, its items can be chosen, a click on the tool bar closes it, and a click on the 3D view closes the menus of the menu bar too. Run under gdb with a breakpoint at `QWindow::requestActivate()`: not called, and no warning (the clicks while the menu stayed open seem to have caused the warning).

**Left to do**
- On Wayland, in full screen, the menus of the menu bar show a little off vertically. Just after going full screen, the first right click's menu also showed off once (2D and 3D; not again). Seemingly a fault of Qt 6.4 on Wayland (on GNOME the application draws the window's decorations); not fixed.

#### 2026-11-17: Step 9-6, the 3D view stopping in full screen (`feature/3d-view`)

The fix of the menus (cbbb392) and the record (aadad3b) were pushed, and the CI passed.

**The user's remark**
- On Wayland, full screen at 4K makes 60 fps. But in the 3D view, full screen with the tool bar and the menu bar hidden (the status bar was hidden already), the view freezes. Only the switch to 2D works, and back in 3D it shows right. Through XWayland it freezes too. Maximized, it does not. In full screen with only the menu bar hidden, it does not.

**Looked into**
- The user ran a tool (in the work's temporary place) that does the steps by timers and writes, every half second, what the 3D view gets (whether it is exposed, the update requests, its size) and the frames it draws.
- While frozen, Qt kept the 3D view exposed (`isExposed()` true), and not one update request (UpdateRequest) reached it. The log kept playing, and the main window called `update()` 10 times a second. Qt does not ask again while the last request waits for the compositor's word to draw the next frame.
- It freezes only when the 3D view covers all of a full screen window (the tool bar, the status bar and the menu bar all hidden). The compositor (GNOME 46) may put a surface covering the whole screen straight on it, without putting the windows together, and then seems to give no word (not confirmed).
- Not drawing with the log stopped is as made (no drawing once the camera stops). At first it looked as if a stopped scene were drawn on while a tool bar or the status bar was shown, but the camera was still moving.
- Tried: with one pixel of the main window left below the 3D view, it does not freeze, on Wayland nor through XWayland. With this tool, it did not freeze through XWayland even without the pixel (it did when the user tried by hand).

**Fixed**
- When the 3D view is shown, the window is full screen and the views' stack (QStackedWidget) covers all of the window, the stack keeps a margin of one pixel at its bottom (`MainWindow::viewMargins()`, `updateViewMargins()`). Looked at again when the stack's size or place changes, when the window's state changes, and when 2D and 3D are switched. The stack is painted black (the pixel of the margin is black).
- The warning at every switch from 2D to 3D, `requestActivate() called for FieldView3D ... which has Qt::WindowDoesNotAcceptFocus set.`: switched from a page with the focus, a QStackedWidget gives the focus to the new page (the 3D view's container), which asks its embedded window to be activated. The focus goes to the views' stack before the switch to 3D. A correction: the last record's "the clicks while the menu stayed open seem to have caused the warning" was wrong; it did not show under gdb because 2D and 3D were not switched then.

**Tests**
- Added to `view_switch`: the margin only with the 3D view, in full screen, with the stack covering all of the window (none with the menu bar, the status bar or a tool bar shown). The 2D view covers all of a full screen window with the bars hidden. The stack is black. Each was checked to fail with what it checks broken (6 ways: always the margin, the 3D view not looked at, full screen not looked at, the stack's size not looked at, not black, the margin in 2D too). The tests without a screen cannot make the 3D view, so the margin with the 3D view and the focus's move are not checked there.

#### 2026-11-17: Step 9-6, Smooth Edges, a goalie without its number, the 3D tab of the settings dialog (`feature/3d-view`)

The fix of the full screen (6529dbb) and the record (f14e236) were pushed, and the CI passed. The user fixes, one by one, what caught the eye while running it (before the documents' review).

**Smooth Edges did nothing**
- The user's remark: turning Smooth Edges on or off changes nothing to be seen.
- The cause: the 3D view draws the HUD (the score board and so on) with a QPainter at the end of every frame. A QPainter that starts painting without antialiasing (`QPainter::Antialiasing`) turns multisampling (`GL_MULTISAMPLE`) off and leaves it so. From the second frame on, drawn into the multisampled framebuffer, every pixel's samples were the same: a picture without smooth edges (since the QOpenGLWindow of 9-6). The test `smoothEdges()` drew no HUD, so it missed it.
- Fixed: the frame's start (`GLFrame::begin()`) turns multisampling back on, with the other states that QPainter may leave.
- Tests: added to `scene_3d` (`smoothEdges()`): a frame after a QPainter as the HUD (with antialiasing and without) is the same as the smooth picture. Checked to fail before the fix.
- The user's check: the edges' stairs change with the option. Full screen on an ultrawide screen (Wayland): 60 fps with smooth edges. Not measured yet on the 4K screen with the edges really smooth.
- A correction: the earlier records' "at 4K, without smooth edges the GPU's time is hardly less" and, on Wayland, "59 to 60 fps even with smooth edges" were with smooth edges not working, by this fault.

**A goalie without its number**
- The user's remark: in the player camera, a goalie whose number is not seen is drawn in the goalie color. By the simulator, without the number it is not known as the goalie either, so it should be in the field players' color.
- Checked: rcssserver tells a player that another is the goalie only with its number.
- Fixed: `playerStyle()` takes how likely a goalie is known to be one (0 to 1; 1 by default). A goalie's color is its team's field color at 0, the goalie color at 1, between them in between. The player camera (`SceneBuilder::addShow()`) gives the number's opacity (as the team colors fade to gray). For the cylinder, the head, the circle on the ground and a figure. A state's color (a tackle and so on) is over it as before. A goalie's catch area fades the same way, none at 0 (even with its directions known). The minimap, which shows everything whatever the sight, keeps the goalie color. 2D and the cameras other than the player camera are as they were.
- Tests: added to `player_style` (0, 0.5, 1, below 0, the body's color, a field player, a state's color), `scene_builder` (the colors of the cylinder, the head and the circle by the perceived number's opacity, a figure's color, the goalie color without the player camera) and `overlay_builder` (the catch area's lines as opaque as the number, none at 0). Each was checked to fail with what it checks broken (6 ways: 3D not giving the number, no clamp at 0, the mix reversed, the catch area not faded, drawn at 0, the number not looked at).
- The user checked it on the screen, the mixing of the colors too.

**The 3D tab of the settings dialog**
- The user's remark: the 3D tab is tall (352×578; the dialog 408×629). Tidy it, even with more tabs.
- Decided (user): the 3D items in three tabs. "3D" (the look of the scene and what is over the view: Compass, Minimap and FPS of Guides, Smooth Edges and Shadows of a new group Rendering, Stadium), "Players & Ball" (Model with Packs and the credits' line, Bodies and Figure Scale of Players, and Ball) and "Camera" (Close-up Camera, Player Camera, Player Sight; the Player Camera tab renamed, with Close-up Camera moved in). Shadows went to Rendering, as they are of the goal frames too, not only of the players. The tab is "Players & Ball", since "Players" does not fit the ball's settings ("Objects" is too wide: the stands and so on are objects too). The web version gets the same shape (§0.2).
- Made: `ConfigDialog::create3DTab()`, `createPlayersBallTab()`, `createCameraTab()`. Each Default sets back only its own tab's items (`Options::setView3DDefaults()`, `setPlayersAndBall3DDefaults()`, `setCamera3DDefaults()`). No scroll buttons, so that every tab's name is seen (`setUsesScrollButtons( false )`). With the cylinders, the empty line of the credits is hidden. The figure packs' documents (`docs/FIGURE_PACKS.md`, `docs/FIGURE_PACKS_MIXAMO.md`) now say Model is on the Players & Ball tab. The settings' names, how they are kept, and the command line's options are as they were.

The menu's name and shortcut (View > Preference, Ctrl+V), the items of the Packs menu, the Players & Ball tab, the catch's heights (0.3 m and 1.5 m), the dive (from 0.8 m to 1.2 m), the steps of turning (a step for 90°, up to 0.75 steps a cycle), and the documents' example against the bundled `figure.json` were right.

**What was fixed**
- `FIGURE_PACKS.md`: turning on the spot's "a standing player turns about 60 degrees in a cycle" became "can turn up to 180 degrees in a cycle, and often turns 60 degrees or more in one" (the numbers looked at in 9-5b: a turn's median 57°, mostly in one cycle). `.fbx` was added to the models' formats in the steps of making a pack.
- "How heavy a pack may be" was added to `FIGURE_PACKS.md`: the GPU moves every vertex of 22 bodies by their bones each frame, and again for the detailed shadows, so its time grows about as a body's triangles (and vertices) do. The table of 9-6's measurement (bodies of 15,000 to 22,000 triangles (the bundled ones): 4 ms with the simple shadows, 7 ms with the detailed; 55,000: 9 ms and 16 ms; 105,000: 18 ms and 30 ms; 420,000: 57 ms and 104 ms). On an integrated GPU, a body of up to about 50,000 triangles with the simple shadows (the default). FPS (the 3D tab, Guides) to check it. Making a body lighter with Blender's Decimate modifier (above the Armature modifier, applied; the bones' weights are kept). Hairstyles not worn cost little. A texture's size matters little for drawing (made smaller above 1024 pixels), but a large file takes longer to read (a pack of 105,000 triangles with textures of 2048 pixels: 0.3 to 0.4 s the first time it is drawn).
- `FIGURE_PACKS_MIXAMO.md`: Mixamo's characters often have more triangles than the bundled bodies (one has 55,000, three times the Human's); if it is slow, keep the shadows simple or make it lighter (pointing to the section above).
- `FIGURE_ASSETS.md`: putting the triangles in order after reading (`GltfModel::orderTriangles()`, Tipsify; the look does not change) was added to "Formats read". A section "Measuring" was added: the items printed with `RCSSMONITOR_3D_TIMING`, an example run with a temporary HOME so that the user's settings file is not written (`--view-3d` takes a value: `--view-3d on`; the example was checked to read the log on a platform without a screen), how to make a heavy pack (a bundled body subdivided in Blender, with textures), comparing times measured at the same time, as an integrated GPU may lower its clock when the work is light, and drawing into an offscreen target to compare the drawing alone, as the GPU's time in a window depends on how the window reaches the screen (XWayland or Wayland).

**Found**
- The copyright shown at the start still says "2009 - 2022". The year is to be updated at the official release (Phase 10).
