#### 2026-11-22: Live connections checked, and the plan of the delayed live view (`feature/3d-view`)

**CI**: with the merge of develop and the distcheck fix (ddd7151, c0c88ba), all five jobs passed. The distcheck job built the 3D view in the tarball's build ("The 3D view is built"), and the 55 tests passed.

**The user's check**: live connections over UDP, TCP and WebSocket, in 2D and 3D. The merge of the TCP / WebSocket support (§9) is done.

**The user's proposal**: the ball flies only in log replay, but it should fly live too. Even when a game runs in real time at a competition, drawing it some seconds behind the game makes it the same as log replay, so the ball can fly. Make a mode that draws some seconds after the frames come.

**Decided (user)**: it comes before Phase 10. Off by default; 3 seconds as a guide. Switched in the settings dialog, on the command line, in the toolbar and in the menu; the dialog and the command line also set the seconds. The largest number of seconds is found from the time the ball takes from its top speed to an almost stop. 2D and 3D are delayed alike. Even without the next touch within the delay, a segment can be cut by the theory of the ball's speed's decay. Written in D15, §5.10 and §9.

**Decided (Claude's proposal, approved by the user)**: "almost stopped" is when the distance the ball still rolls is shorter than the kickable distance (0.065 m/cycle with the default server, 6.2 seconds from the top speed). The seconds range from 1 to 7 in steps of 0.5. The cut goes into log replay too (§5.10).

**Looked into**: live frames are kept (`DispHolder::MAX_LIVE_FRAMES`). `BallFlight` finds the last segment from the last frame at hand even when it is open, so the delayed live view needs open segments fixed on the ground. The time to an almost stop was computed with the default server's values (`ball_speed_max` 3.0, `ball_decay` 0.94, rcssserver's `serverparam.cpp`) (§5.10).

#### 2026-11-22: The delayed live view (1): the delayed clock, the views and the options (`feature/3d-view`)

The user approved the proposal of §5.10 and asked to go on.

**Made**
- `Options`: `live_delay` (off by default) and `live_delay_seconds` (3 by default). `setLiveDelaySeconds()` keeps them within 1 to 7 seconds, rounded to steps of 0.5 (`MIN_LIVE_DELAY_SECONDS`, `MAX_LIVE_DELAY_SECONDS`, `LIVE_DELAY_SECONDS_STEP`, `DEFAULT_LIVE_DELAY_SECONDS`; the reason of the largest in the header's comment). Saved at the end. On the command line, `--live-delay SECONDS` (0 turns it off; a negative value or not a number ends with an error).
- `LogPlayer`: `liveDelayFrames()` (the seconds divided by the server's `simulator_step`), `isDelayedLive()`, `liveIndex()` (the frame the 2D view shows: the delay before the newest, going on by the steps since the newest came, up to the newest when a frame is late; the first frame with no more frames than the delay), `receivedFrame()` (the time a frame came, and shown if live), `applyLiveDelay()` (the options changed: jump if live). The delay's timer (25 ms) moves the shown frame on by the clock. As the frames come and by the clock it only goes on (frames coming together after a late one do not take it back); it jumps only when switched. When no longer live, the timer stops.
- `Interpolate::liveTime()` takes the delay in frames (the larger of it and LIVE_DELAY). `waitingLive()`. `FieldView3D` passes `LogPlayer::liveDelayFrames()`, and draws the first frame while waiting.
- `MainWindow`: a checkable action "Live Delay" (a clock icon, `DrawnIcon::LiveDelay`; the seconds in the tooltip; the referee's operations in the status line), after Live Mode in the toolbar and in the Monitor menu. What comes from the server goes to `LogPlayer::receivedFrame()`. In the delayed live view, the toolbar's time field shows "LIVE -3.0 s".
- `ConfigDialog`: "Live" on the Show tab, with Delay and Seconds (1 to 7 s, steps of 0.5; a value between the steps is shown again at the nearest step). `liveDelayChanged()` tells the main window, and `updateLiveDelay()` shows the toolbar's switch.

**Tests**
- Test `log_player` (new): the range and the rounding of the option, `liveIndex()`, the delay in frames (changing with the server's step), following the frames (waiting, frames coming together, not back after moving on by the clock), switching (on: back by the delay; the seconds changed; off: to the newest; the Live Mode button back to the delayed view), drawing to the end after the frames stop (a fast server, 10 ms), and not moving when not live.
- `interpolate` got the delayed `liveTime()` and the waiting, and that the 2D view's frame and the 3D view's time agree (`LogPlayer::liveIndex()` and `liveTime()` rounded down). `toolbar` got the clock icon, the button's place, the menu, the option, the time field, and following the dialog's changes. `config_dialog` got the Live fields (the tab, the range, the step, set with Enter, rounded to the step, showing a change made elsewhere without telling it again).
- Each was checked to fail with the code broken for a while (10 ways: not going back, 3D using the delay, rounding to the step, drawing to the end, jumping when switched, the server's step, the menu, the time field, the dialog's rounding, the action following the dialog).
- The command line (with a temporary HOME): `--live-delay abc` and `-1` end with an error. `2.3` turns it on with 2.5 seconds, saved at the end. `0` turns it off.
- rcssserver's develop ran with a temporary `RCSS_CONF_DIR` and ports other than the usual ones, measured by a program drawing at 60 Hz with `MonitorClient`, `LogPlayer` and the 3D view's clock (UDP, TCP and WebSocket with 3 seconds; UDP with 7, TCP with 1): the 2D view is always exactly the delay's frames behind the newest (30 with 3 seconds). The 3D view is 29.46 ± 0.29 behind (0.02 to 1.06 from the 2D view's frame: it draws between the frames). The 3D time goes on 0.160 ± 0.005 cycles a drawing (as without the delay), and neither view goes back. With the connection closed, both drew to the last frame in the delay's seconds (2.9 s with 3, 6.9 s with 7, 0.9 s with 1).
- In the first measurement, the 3D time went back 9 times while waiting right after connecting (the clock ran 2 frames past a target that did not go on, and jumped back). With the first frame drawn without the clock while waiting, it became 0.
- With Qt 6.4.2 and Qt 5.15.13 (`-Werror=deprecated-declarations`), the build and the 56 tests pass. `-DENABLE_3D=OFF` (22; `config_dialog` skipped as before) and autotools (Qt5) pass too.

#### 2026-11-22: The delayed live view (2): the ball flying (`feature/3d-view`)

**The user's check (after step (1))**: with the delay on, the view was drawn 3 seconds behind. The ball did not fly live (step (1) kept `BallFlight` off live; making it fly is step (2), which had not been explained well enough). Step (2) followed at once.

**Made**
- `BallFlight`:
  - The cut at the almost stop: from the ball's move in the cycle after a kick, the cycles until it rolls no farther than the kickable distance (`kickableDistance()`: `player_size` + `kickable_margin` + `ball_size`) are found (`stopCycles()`: 59 from 2.5 m/cycle, 62 from 3.0). Without a touch in them, the segment is cut there (`Touch::Stop`, reach 0: on the ground). The segment after it is not lofted. It applies to log replay too. It does not cut while the frames do not reach there yet (live, still coming).
  - The delayed live view: `update()` takes the frame drawn (`live_drawn`; `NOT_LIVE` in log replay). If the end of the kick's segment it is in is not known yet (`decided()`: the last segment, or one ending at a collision whose following touch (a trap) is not known yet), the segment is fixed on the ground (`Segment::grounded`, remembered by the time and the stopped time of its first frame). It stays on the ground when the touch comes later, and when the view is no longer live and looks back. Other frames (another log or game) forget it.
  - When frames are only added, the heights of the segments the same as before (frames, ends, reach, starting height, grounding) are kept, and only the last ones are found again (`loft()` tries many upward speeds). When a segment is grounded, only it and those after it are found again.
- `FrameBuilder3D::setLive()` takes the delay in frames. In the delayed live view it passes the frame drawn, and the ball flies. A live view without the delay stays on the ground as before. `FieldView3D` passes `LogPlayer::liveDelayFrames()`.
- The tooltip of Flight in the settings dialog and the command line's help tell that the ball flies in the delayed live view too (a segment whose touch has not come stays on the ground).

**Tests**
- Added to the test `ball_flight_3d`: the cut (the number of cycles and its formula, the segment cut where the ball stops, on the ground, the segment after it not lofted, no cut with a touch before, no cut while the frames do not reach there), the delayed live view (a segment entered before its end is known is on the ground, also after the touch comes and when looked back at, also when the view moves into it on the same frames; one that ended before it is drawn has log replay's heights; forgotten by `clear()` and by another game), and `decided()` (the last segment; after a collision until a trap is known). `frame_builder_3d`: the ball flies in the delayed live view.
- Each was checked to fail with the code broken for a while (9 ways: no cut, the cut's reach, the cycles off by one, no grounding, grounding ended segments too, not grounding on the same frames, keeping the grounding for another game, ignoring the trap, no flight in the delayed live view).
- The delayed live view was emulated on 4 real games (the frames came one by one, `BallFlight` was updated at each frame drawn by `LogPlayer::liveIndex()`, and the heights drawn were compared with log replay's; at the end the frames stopped and were drawn to the end):
  - The cut at the almost stop never happened in the 4 games (someone always touched the ball before it stopped). Log replay of these 4 games does not change.
  - With 3 (and 7) seconds: 1 to 3 kick segments a game were grounded (0 or 1 of them lofted). The frames in the air were 99.3 to 100% of log replay's. The other differences in height come from the grounded segments and the low lofted kicks after them (small hops while dribbling) that carry on the height where the segment before ended (up to about 0.35 m, for a few seconds).
  - The frames in the air, of log replay's: 62 to 82% with 1 second, 81 to 97% with 1.5, 90 to 98% with 2, 99.3 to 100% with 3 or more. The guide of 3 seconds is almost the same as log replay.
  - The time of an update: at first 6 to 7 ms each near the end of a game (the table built again; in the drawing, every 100 ms). With the heights of the same segments kept, the worst is under 1 ms, the mean 0.14 to 0.2 ms. The kept heights are the same as those built again (no difference in the segments not grounded).

CMake first, autotools last, with a table of contents. One file is enough, so it was not split (about 280 lines).

Quick Start (the table of the Qt6 packages by Ubuntu's release, the two `cmake` commands, `cmake-helper.sh`'s main commands), and Installing and Uninstalling made short, pointing to `docs/build.md` for the details. - The README's Qt6 packages lacked Qt6's OpenGL development package. On Ubuntu 24.04, OpenGL and OpenGLWidgets are in `qt6-base-dev` (checked on the work machine; 24.04 has no `libqt6opengl6-dev`), but 22.04 and 26.04 need `libqt6opengl6-dev`, as in the CI (and `libgl-dev` on 22.04). Following the README, the 3D view was likely not built on 22.04 and 26.04. It is now a table by release.
